1. Recommender Systems — Architecture Diagrams

01. The Canonical Multi-Stage Recommender Architecture

1.1 The cost argument

                 RETRIEVAL (recall, fast, coarse)    RANKING (precision, slow, rich)
              +---------------------------------+-----------------------------------+
   OFFLINE    | train embedding / two-tower     | train ranking model on logged     |
   (batch)    | compute item vectors            | impressions + labels              |
              | build ANN / i2i / graph indices | build feature tables / store      |
              +---------------------------------+-----------------------------------+
   ONLINE     | embed user/query, ANN lookup,   | hydrate features for ~10^2-10^3   |
   (request)  | graph walks, rules, merge       | candidates, score, re-rank, serve |
              +---------------------------------+-----------------------------------+
                        (after Yan, 2021 [5])

2.2 Architecture

                         +----------------------- OFFLINE -------------------------+
   member events  --->   | Chukwa -> Hadoop/Hive/Pig -> model training               |
   (plays, ratings,      |   -> batch-computed recs / model params                  |
    browsing)            +---------------------------|-------------------------------+
        |                                            v
        |                +---------------------- NEARLINE ------------------------+
        +-- Manhattan -> | event-triggered compute: e.g. solve user factors,      |
           (event flow)  | dot products, refresh cached scores                    |
                         +---------------------------|-------------------------------+
                                                     v
                              result stores (relational / Cassandra / EVCache)
                                                     |
                         +---------------------- ONLINE --------------------------+
   page request  ----->  | context filtering, light model eval, merge, fallbacks  | -> UI
                         +--------------------------------------------------------+
                                    (after Amatriain & Basilico, 2013 [1])

3.2 Architecture

  video corpus           candidate generation          ranking             top-N
  (millions)   ------->  (deep net over user history ->  (deep net,       -> (dozens)
                          extreme multiclass softmax;     rich features,
  other candidate ---->   ANN search at serving)          watch-time
  sources                 => hundreds                     objective)
                                     (after Covington et al., 2016 [3][15])

4.1 Instagram Explore, 2019: the three-pass distillation funnel

  ig2vec account KNN + other sources
            |
            v
   ~500 candidates
            |   pass 1: distillation model (minimal features) mimics later stages
            v
   ~150 candidates
            |   pass 2: lightweight neural network
            v
   ~50 candidates
            |   pass 3: full deep neural network (all dense features)
            v
   ~25 shown first (after integrity/diversity rules)
                                 (after Instagram, 2019 [7]; counts approximate)

4.2 Instagram Explore, 2023: four stages, caching everywhere

   +-------------+   up to   +-----------------+   top    +-----------------+   +--------+
   | Retrieval   |  ~1,500   | Early-stage     |  ~100    | Late-stage      |-->| Final  |
   | heuristics, |---------->| ranking (two-   |--------->| ranking (MTML,  |   | rerank |
   | two-tower   |           | tower, distilled|          | value model)    |   | rules, |
   | ANN, ...    |           | from LSR)       |          |                 |   | divers.|
   +-------------+           +-----------------+          +-----------------+   +--------+
     item tower: daily offline embeddings; user tower: online, fresh features
                                     (after Meta 2023 [8] and transparency page [9])

5.1 2023 architecture

  request -> Home Mixer (Product Mixer framework)
               |
               +-- in-network:  Earlybird index + light ranker (RealGraph) --+
               +-- out-of-net:  UTEG/GraphJet, SimClusters ANN, TwHIN ANN ---+--> ~1,500
               |                                                             |
               +--> feature hydration --> Heavy Ranker (~48M param MTL NN) <-+
               |
               +--> visibility filtering, author diversity, dedup, fatigue
               +--> mix ads / WTF modules --> For You timeline
                                     (after Twitter 2023 [11][12])

6. Pinterest: candidate generators, a light-weight scorer and a blender

  Pixie walks | HNSW embeds | token idx | explore  --> merge+dedup --> LWS (light)
                                                                       |
                                     Utility Prediction Scorer (MTL) <-+
                                                 |
                                Multi-Objective Blender --> home feed
                                    (after Pinterest WWW 2022 [14], [16])

8.3 Kuaishou: a six-stage cascade, quantified

   corpus (10^8+)
      | retrieval: many channels (i2i, u2i, transformer retrieval e.g. KuaiFormer)
      v
   ~8,000 merged candidates
      | pre-ranking / coarse ranking
      v
   ~500
      | cascade ranking -> full (fine) ranking (multi-task)
      v
   ~tens
      | re-ranking (listwise, diversity, business)
      v
   ~6-10 exposed
                              (after RecFlow [22], UniRec [23]; approximate)

10. Request flow, end to end

  client --> API/gateway --> mixer/orchestrator (per-surface pipeline)
     1. fetch user context: profile, recent actions (real-time feature store)
     2. fan out to N candidate sources in parallel, each with its own timeout
          two-tower ANN | i2i / graph walk | follow graph | trending | rules
     3. merge, dedup, eligibility & integrity filter          (~10^3-10^4)
     4. pre-rank: cached / distilled / two-tower or small DNN (-> ~10^2-10^3)
     5. hydrate rich features (batch RPC to feature store, embeddings)
     6. rank: multi-task NN -> P(event_i); value model sum w_i * P_i (-> ~10^1-10^2)
     7. re-rank: diversity, freshness, fatigue, policy, ads/module insertion
     8. log impressions + features served ("log-and-wait") for training
     9. respond; async side effects (cache next page, update seen-sets)

02. Candidate Generation and Retrieval: Two-Tower Models, Negative Sampling, ANN Indexes and Multi-Source Retrieval

Overview

                    THE CANONICAL RETRIEVAL FUNNEL
 corpus: 1e6 .. 1e10 items
   |
   |  [Retrieval / candidate generation]   many sources in parallel
   |    - two-tower EBR (ANN over item embeddings)
   |    - item-to-item CF / co-engagement tables
   |    - graph walks (Pixie, GraphJet/UTEG, PPR)
   |    - social graph (friends-of-friends, in-network)
   |    - heuristic (trending, fresh, location)
   v
 ~1e3 .. 1e4 candidates (dedup + merge + filter)
   |
   |  [Pre-rank / lightweight scoring]  often a two-tower too
   v
 ~1e2 .. 1e3
   |
   |  [Heavy ranking]  multi-task DNN with cross features
   v
 ~1e1 .. 1e2 shown, after re-ranking for diversity, policy, ads

1.1 YouTube DNN candidate generation (Google, 2016)

  YouTube DNN candidate generation (2016)
  user history + context features --> MLP (ReLU stack) --> user vector u
                                                              |
  training:  softmax over all videos (approximated by sampled softmax)
  serving :  top-N by dot(u, v_j) via nearest-neighbor index over v_j

1.2 The two-tower model with in-batch negatives and logQ correction (YouTube, 2019)

  Two-tower training with in-batch softmax + logQ   (batch of B pairs)

  query tower  q_i = f(user_i, context_i)          i = 1..B
  item tower   v_j = g(item_j features)            j = 1..B
  logits       s_ij = <q_i, v_j> / temperature  -  log p_j     (logQ)
  loss         -sum_i log softmax_j(s_ij)[j = i]
  p_j          streaming estimate from hashed "last-seen step" arrays

1.6 Facebook Search EBR: triplet loss, hard negatives and ANN tuning (KDD 2020)

  Facebook EBR inside Unicorn (2020)

  query + searcher context --> query tower --> q (e.g. 128-d)
                                                   |
            Unicorn query:  (nn  q :radius ...)  AND  (term filters)
                                                   |
  inverted index:  term = coarse cluster id (IVF centroid)
                   payload = PQ code of residual
                                                   |
             candidates --> later-stage ranking (embedding as a feature)

2.1 Instagram Explore retrieval (2023)

  Instagram Explore (2023, per Meta)
   sources (weighted mix): heuristic | item-CF | PPR | two-tower | ...
        |  hundreds each, from billions
        v
   retrieval output: <= ~1500 media
        |  first-stage ranker = two-tower (cached user + item embeddings)
        v
   top 100 --> second-stage ranker (heavy MTML) --> final re-rank

Scale.

  Pixie (graph retriever)
   query pins (weighted) --> N short random walks w/ restart on Pin<->Board graph
                              (single-machine RAM, ~120 GB pruned graph)
                         --> visit counts (early stop when top-k stabilizes)
                         --> top-k pins as candidates   (p99 < 60 ms)

4.1 LiNR: model-based neural retrieval on GPUs (2024)

  LiNR (GPU "retrieval as a model")
   model binary = { user/query tower weights , item matrix [N x d] , attrs }
   request:  q = tower(member)          (GPU)
             mask = prefilter(attrs)    (GPU, exact)
             scores = q . items^T  over masked rows  (dense matmul / quantized)
             top-k --> ranking

5. Twitter/X: a portfolio of graph and embedding retrievers

  Twitter "For You" candidate sourcing (2023)
   in-network  : follow graph --> RealGraph-weighted (LR) ---------\
   out-network : UTEG/GraphJet traversal ---------------------------+--> ~1500
                 SimClusters sparse community vectors -------------|     merged
                 TwHIN dense KG embeddings (ANN) ------------------/     to ranker

10. Multi-source retrieval and merging

  Generic multi-source retrieval service
  request --> fan-out (parallel, per-source timeout)
     |-- EBR source(s): user tower -> ANN (k per embedding) -----\
     |-- item-CF: recent items -> co-engagement table lookups ---|
     |-- graph walk: seeds -> random walks / traversals ---------+--> union
     |-- social: follow / FoF expansion ---------------------------|   dedup
     |-- heuristic: trending / fresh / local ----------------------/   filter
                                                                        |
                       quotas / weights / round-robin / pre-ranker ------+
                                                                        v
                                                         1e2..1e3 to ranking

03. The Evolution of Ranking and CTR Models: From Logistic Regression to Scaling-Law Architectures

Overview

Evolution of CTR / ranking architectures (simplified)

2010  FM (Rendle) ---------------------------------------+
2013  LR + FTRL-Proximal (Google ads)                    |
2014  GBDT -> LR (Facebook ads)      FFM (Kaggle/Criteo) |
2016  Wide & Deep (Google Play)                          |
2017  DeepFM (Huawei)  DCN (Google)  DIN (Alibaba) <-----+ factorized / attention
2018  xDeepFM (CIN)  AutoInt (self-attn)  DIEN (AUGRU)
2019  DLRM (Facebook, open source)
2020  DCN-V2 (Google)  SIM (Alibaba, 54k-length sequences)
2021  MaskNet (Weibo) -> Twitter heavy ranker (2023)
2022  DHEN (Meta)
2023  TWIN (Kuaishou)
2024  Wukong (Meta)  HSTU (Meta)  LiRank (LinkedIn)  InterFormer (Meta)
2025  RankMixer (ByteDance)  GEM (Meta ads foundation model)
2026  TokenMixer-Large (ByteDance)  ML-DCN (Pinterest)  GEM training at LLM scale

Architecture and algorithm

 impression stream --> feature extraction --> hashed / sparse feature ids
                                                   |
                                                   v
                        +-------------------------------------------+
                        |  FTRL-Proximal logistic regression        |
                        |  per-coordinate state: z_i, n_i           |
                        |  L1 => exact zeros => sparse model        |
                        +-------------------------------------------+
                                  |                     |
                       weights (q2.13, 16-bit)   calibration layer
                                  |                     |
                                  v                     v
                             serving replicas  -->  pCTR to auction

Architecture

 raw features (dense + categorical)
          |
          v
 +-----------------------------+     trained in batch (e.g. daily)
 |  Boosted trees T1 ... TK    |
 +-----------------------------+
   | leaf id   | leaf id    | leaf id
   v           v            v
 [one-hot(T1) | one-hot(T2) | ... | one-hot(TK)]  + other sparse features
          |
          v
 +-----------------------------+     trained online, near real time
 | Logistic regression (SGD)   |
 +-----------------------------+
          |
          v
     pCTR  --> recalibrate for negative downsampling --> auction

4.1 Wide & Deep (Google Play, 2016)

      wide (memorization)                     deep (generalization)
 sparse ids + cross products         sparse ids -> embeddings (~32-d each)
            |                                  + dense features
            |                                     |
            |                               MLP (ReLU layers)
            |                                     |
            +----------- sum of logits -----------+
                               |
                            sigmoid -> P(app acquisition)

4.6 DLRM (Facebook/Meta, 2019)

 dense features --> bottom MLP --> d-dim vector ----+
                                                    |
 sparse feat 1 --> embedding table 1 --> d-dim -----+
 sparse feat 2 --> embedding table 2 --> d-dim -----+--> pairwise dot products
 ...                                                |    (all pairs of vectors)
 sparse feat N --> embedding table N --> d-dim -----+          |
                                                               v
                               concat(dot products, bottom-MLP output)
                                                               |
                                                           top MLP
                                                               |
                                                           sigmoid

5.1 DIN: Deep Interest Network (KDD 2018)

 candidate ad emb ------------------------------+
                                                |
 behavior_1 emb --[act unit(b1, ad)]-- w1 --+   |
 behavior_2 emb --[act unit(b2, ad)]-- w2 --+-> sum_i w_i * b_i --> user interest
 ...                                        |                         |
 behavior_T emb --[act unit(bT, ad)]-- wT --+                         |
                                                                       v
           concat(user interest, user profile, ad, context) --> MLP --> pCTR

5.3 SIM: Search-based Interest Model (CIKM 2020)

 lifelong behaviors (up to ~54,000)
          |
          v
 +------------------------------------------+
 | General Search Unit (GSU)                |
 |   hard search: behaviors with same       |
 |     category as candidate (index lookup) |
 |   soft search: embedding MIPS            |
 +------------------------------------------+
          | top-K relevant behaviors (K ~ hundreds)
          v
 +------------------------------------------+
 | Exact Search Unit (ESU)                  |
 |   multi-head target attention            |
 +------------------------------------------+
          |
          v
   concat with short-term DIN/DIEN features --> MLP --> pCTR

6.1 DHEN: Deep and Hierarchical Ensemble Network (Meta, 2022)

 feature embeddings
       |
 +-----------------------------------------------+
 | Layer 1: [DotProd] [SelfAttn] [DCN] [Linear]  |  -> ensemble combine + residual
 +-----------------------------------------------+
       |
 +-----------------------------------------------+
 | Layer 2: [DotProd] [SelfAttn] [DCN] [Linear]  |
 +-----------------------------------------------+
       |
      ...  -> top MLP -> task heads

6.5 Meta GEM (2025-2026)

              +-----------------------------------------+
              |  GEM foundation model (offline, huge)   |
              |  sequence + non-sequence interaction    |
              +-----------------------------------------+
                  |  knowledge transfer / distillation
        +---------+-----------+-----------------+
        v                     v                 v
  vertical model A      vertical model B   vertical model C   (served online,
  (e.g. surface/CVR)    (e.g. CTR)         (...)              latency-bounded)

04. Multi-Task and Multi-Objective Ranking: From Shared-Bottom to Value Models

Overview

                 +--------------------------------------------+
  features  ---> |  multi-task ranker (shared-bottom / MMoE / |
  (user, item,   |  PLE / MaskNet / transformer)              |
   context)      +--------------------------------------------+
                   | pClick | pLike | pShare | pHide | E[dwell] ...
                   v        v       v        v       v
                 +--------------------------------------------+
                 | calibration (per task: Platt / temperature |
                 | / isotonic / learned isotonic layer)       |
                 +--------------------------------------------+
                                  |
                                  v
                 +--------------------------------------------+
                 | value model / fusion:                      |
                 |   score = sum_i w_i * p_i   (linear)       |
                 |   score = prod_i p_i ^ w_i  (log-linear)   |
                 |   w = f(user, request)      (personalized) |
                 +--------------------------------------------+
                                  |
                                  v
                 diversity / out-of-network / freshness
                 multipliers, DPP rerank, integrity demotions

1.1 Shared-bottom (hard parameter sharing)

  input embeddings -> [shared MLP] -> h
                                    |-> tower_click -> pClick
                                    |-> tower_like  -> pLike
                                    |-> tower_share -> pShare

1.2 MMoE: Multi-gate Mixture-of-Experts (Google, KDD 2018)

                    +----------+  +----------+       +----------+
  x ------------->  | expert 1 |  | expert 2 |  ...  | expert n |
  |                 +----------+  +----------+       +----------+
  |                        \            |            /
  |-> gate_A (softmax) ---> weighted sum_A --> tower_A --> y_A
  |-> gate_B (softmax) ---> weighted sum_B --> tower_B --> y_B

1.3 YouTube "What Video to Watch Next" (RecSys 2019)

  user/video/context features --> [MMoE: experts + per-objective gates]
                                        |               |
                                  engagement towers  satisfaction towers
                                        |               |
  position, device -> [shallow tower] --+ (bias logit added at train only)
                                        v
              per-objective predictions --> weighted combination --> rank

Architecture.

  Level 1:  [A-experts] [shared-experts] [B-experts]
              gate_A      gate_shared      gate_B
                |              |              |
  Level 2:  [A-experts] [shared-experts] [B-experts]
              gate_A                       gate_B
                |                             |
             tower_A -> VTR (AUC)          tower_B -> VCR (MSE)

1.7 Sequential-dependency models: ESMM and successors

  impression features
        |
   [shared embeddings]
     /            \
  [CTR tower]   [CVR tower]
     |               |
   pCTR ----(x)---- pCVR
     |         |
  loss(click)  pCTCVR -> loss(click & conversion)

2.2 X / xAI (2026): Phoenix transformer and the xai-value-model

  Phoenix (transformer, candidate-isolated) -> ~25 heads
        -> weighted sum (param.rs weights) -> offset negatives
        -> + exploration bonus -> x author-diversity decay
        -> x out-of-network factor -> cold-start lift
        -> vm-ranker DPP rerank -> filters -> timeline

2.8 Kuaishou: from formula fusion to learned ensemble sort

  ranking model --> pLTR, pWTR, pFTR, pCMTR, E[watch] ...
                          |
     +--------------------+--------------------+
     | 2018-era: hand formula, global weights  |
     | 2022+:    RL agent picks weights/user   |
     | 2025+:    Pareto policy trained jointly |
     +--------------------+--------------------+
                          v
                   top-k by fused score

5.1 The workflow most companies converge on

  1. Train calibrated MTL heads (one model, many heads).
  2. Define the value function: score = sum w_i * p_i (+ multipliers).
  3. Initialize w_i ~ 1 / mean(p_i) * importance_i  (equal-contribution
     heuristic, as in Twitter 2023 [11]).
  4. Offline: replay logged sessions; sweep w; trace a Pareto front of
     proxy metrics (e.g. predicted likes vs predicted hides) [21][32].
  5. Online: constrained Bayesian optimization or GP Thompson sampling
     over w, primary metric s.t. guardrails [22][26].
  6. Personalize: learn w = f(user, request) with RL / policy
     optimization, keep a Pareto frontier for governance [32][35][36].
  7. Re-check calibration on every model change; re-tune w on every
     product-goal change.

05. Sequential and User-History Modeling: From GRU4Rec to Lifelong Sequences and Foundation User Models

Overview

                     The user-history design space
  +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
  |                 short (10^1-10^2)    long (10^3-10^4)    lifelong (10^5+) |
  | retrieval       GRU4Rec, SASRec,     PinnerFormer,       PinFM, HSTU,     |
  | (user tower)    BERT4Rec, KuaiFormer Netflix FM          Netflix FM       |
  | ranking         DIN, BST, TransAct   MIMN, LONGER,       SIM, ETA, SDIM,  |
  | (target-aware)                       TransAct V2         TWIN, TWIN V2    |
  +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
     cost grows with length x candidates x model depth -> most tricks attack this

Architecture.

  other features (user profile, context, cross)        target item
          |                                                 |
     embeddings                     behaviour seq: item_1 .. item_n
          |                                 |  (item_id, category_id,
          |                                 |   position = t_now - t_click)
          |                         +-------v-------------------+
          |                         | Transformer layer          |
          |                         | (self-attn over seq+target)|
          |                         +-------+-------------------+
          +-------------+-------------------+
                        |  concat
                   MLP (3 layers) -> sigmoid -> pCTR

3.2 SIM: Search-based Interest Model (Alibaba, CIKM 2020)

 lifelong behaviours (10^4 - 10^5)
          |
 +--------v-------------------------------+
 | GSU: General Search Unit                |
 |  hard search: behaviours with the same  |
 |   category as the candidate             |   offline: User Behavior Tree
 |  soft search: MIPS on embeddings        |<--  key1 = user_id
 +--------+-------------------------------+     key2 = category_id
          | top-K sub-sequence (SBS)            value = behaviour items
 +--------v-------------------------------+     (distributed, ~22 TB)
 | ESU: Exact Search Unit                  |
 |  multi-head target attention on SBS     |
 |  + time-interval embeddings             |
 +--------+-------------------------------+
          v
   concat with short-term DIN features -> MLP -> pCTR

3.4 TWIN: TWo-stage Interest Network (Kuaishou, KDD 2023)

  per behaviour j:  K_j = W * inherent_j     (cached offline per item)
                    b_j = w . cross_j         (cheap, per user)
  per candidate c:  score_j = (Q(c) . K_j) / sqrt(d) + b_j
  CP-GSU: top-100 by score_j  ->  ESU: multi-head attention over top-100

Architecture.

 last N actions (pin, action type, surface, time features)
      |  each pin: 256-d PinSage embedding (frozen) + learned feature embeddings
 +----v------------------------------+
 | causal Transformer encoder         |
 +----+------------------------------+
      v
 user embedding (L2-normalised), computed daily for every user
      |
      v  ANN retrieval / ranking feature, matched against pin embeddings

4.3 TransAct (KDD 2023): real-time actions inside the ranker

   real-time action stream (Kafka-like)                  candidate pin
          |                                                    |
   latest ~100 actions: PinSage emb + action type + time       |
          |                                                    |
   early fusion: concat each action with candidate embedding <-+
          |
   Transformer encoder (small: ~2 layers, reported)
          |
   output compression: first K tokens + max pooling -> flatten
          |
   concat with PinnerFormer user emb + other features -> DCN-style ranker
          |
   multi-head outputs (repin, click, hide, ...) -> utility score

Architecture.

  lifelong seq (~16k, int8 PinSage, offline store)  real-time seq   impression seq
          \                                           |             /
           +------- nearest-neighbour selection by candidate -------+
                   (dot product in PinSage space; always keep
                    the most recent real-time actions)
                              |
                      short selected sequence
                              |
                Transformer (fused single-kernel)
                              |
              features -> ranker heads  +  Next Action Loss (aux)

8.1 Split the user part from the candidate part

  request -> [ user history encoder ]  ---- once per request (or cached / batch)
                    |   KV cache / embedding
          +---------+----------+----------+
          v         v          v          v
       cand_1    cand_2  ...  cand_1000      cheap cross-attention or dot product

06. Generative Recommenders (2023-2026): HSTU, Semantic IDs, OneRec, MTGR and the Foundation-Model Turn

1. The conceptual shift: from DLRM to generative recommenders

 Classical cascade (DLRM era)            Generative recommender (GR era)
 ----------------------------            --------------------------------
 user feats ─┐                           [item0, act0, item1, act1, ... , itemN]
 item feats ─┼─> Retrieval (two-tower)              │ causal transformer (HSTU,
 cross feats ┘      │ ~10^3-10^4                     │  decoder-only, enc-dec)
                    v                                v
             Pre-rank (light DNN)        retrieval head: p(next item | history)
                    │ ~10^2-10^3         ranking head:  p(action | history, item)
                    v                    or: decode semantic-ID tokens directly
             Rank (DLRM, MTL)                        │
                    │ ~10^1-10^2                     v
                    v                    (optional) RL post-training vs reward model
             Re-rank / blend

2.3 The HSTU block

   X ──> Linear ──> SiLU ──> split ──> U, V, Q, K
                                      │
            SiLU(Q·K^T + rab(pos,time)) ⊙ V      <- no softmax; rel. pos+time bias
                                      │
                       LayerNorm(·) ⊙ U          <- gating replaces the FFN
                                      │
                            Linear ──> + X (residual)

3.2 Pipeline

 item text/metadata ──> frozen text encoder (e.g., Sentence-T5) ──> dense emb e
                                                                    │
                       RQ-VAE: encoder -> z
                       level 1: c1 = argmin ||z - C1[k]||, r1 = z - C1[c1]
                       level 2: c2 = argmin ||r1 - C2[k]||, r2 = r1 - C2[c2]
                       level 3: c3 = ...
                                                                    │
                    Semantic ID = (c1, c2, c3 [, dedup token])  e.g. (10, 5, 42)
                                                                    │
 user history = [SID(item1), SID(item2), ...] ──> Transformer seq2seq (T5-style)
                                                ──> beam-decode SID of next item
                                                ──> map SID -> item (lookup)

Tokenizer.

 user static ─┐
 short seq 20 ├─> Encoder (Transformer, RMSNorm) ──> 405 ctx tokens
 pos-fb 256  ─┤                                          │ cross-attn
 lifelong 1e5 ─> hier-kmeans 2000 ─> QFormer 128 ─┘      v
                         [BOS] s1 s2 s3 ──> Decoder (causal + MoE FFN)
                                                         │ beam search over SIDs
                                                         v
                       candidate sessions ──> Reward model (P-Score: CTR/LTR/VTR..)
                                                         │
                         preference alignment (DPO-style IPA in V1 paper;
                         ECPO = early-clipped GRPO in technical report)

5.2 Architecture

 [U1..Uk | S1 ... Sn | R1 ... Rr | C1  C2  ...  Cm]
   static   long seq   real-time   candidates (item ⊕ cross feats)
 mask: U,S -> visible to all; R causal & time-gated per candidate; Ci ⟂ Cj
 heads: CTR / CTCVR on C positions only

7.2 Design elements (as described in the blog; summarized via a public reimplementation's mapping)

 member events ──> tokenizer (merge adjacent actions) ──> FM transformer
                                                         │   (sparse attn,
                                                         │    multi-token heads)
                    ┌────────────────────┬───────────────┴──────────┐
                    v                    v                          v
          direct prediction      user/item embeddings        fine-tuned task models
          (next-title scores)    (stable via projection)     (rows, search, notifs)

10. X (Twitter) For You: Phoenix transformer ranker

 viewer engagement seq ─┐
 Thunder (in-network)  ─┼─> candidates ─> filters ─> Phoenix transformer
 Phoenix ANN (OON)     ─┤                         (cands attend only to viewer ctx)
 SimClusters / popular ─┘                                 │ P(fav), P(reply), ...
                                                          v
                      Σ w_i P(a_i) ─> diversity/OON adj ─> DPP rerank ─> VF ─> blend

07. Graph-Based Recommendation in Production: Random Walks, Graph Embeddings and GNNs at Web Scale

1. A taxonomy of graph recommenders in production

                    where is the graph computation done?
                 request time                  offline / nearline
              +-----------------------+    +------------------------------+
  no learned  | Pixie, GraphJet,      |    | SimClusters (community       |
  params      | SALSA/WTF, 2-hop walk |    | detection + sparse vectors), |
              | (walks over in-memory |    | Tweepcred PageRank,          |
              |  graph)               |    | RealGraph aggregates         |
              +-----------------------+    +------------------------------+
  learned     | AliGraph DGS (sample  |    | PinSage, LiGNN, Uber Eats    |
  params      |  subgraph online, GNN |    | GraphSAGE (inductive GNN);   |
              |  forward in TF-Serve) |    | EGES, TwHIN, PBG (shallow    |
              |                       |    | embeddings) -> ANN index     |
              +-----------------------+    +------------------------------+

Architecture

 user actions (saves, clicks)      offline graph build (Hadoop, daily)
          |                                   |
          v                                   v
  query pins Q={q1..qk}, weights w_q    pruned pin<->board graph
          |                          (CSR arrays, loaded into RAM)
          v                                   |
  +--------------------------- Pixie server --+-----------------+
  | for each q in Q: budget N_q ~ w_q * f(degree(q))            |
  |   repeat: walk q -> board -> pin -> board ... (biased       |
  |           by user language/topic), restart w.p. alpha       |
  |   count visits V_q[p]; early-stop when n_p pins have >= n_v |
  | combine: V[p] = (sum_q sqrt(V_q[p]))^2   (multi-hit boost)  |
  +------------------------------------------+------------------+
                                             v
                            top-K pins -> ranking model -> surface

Architecture

            TRAINING (GPU, producer-consumer)
  CPU producers: sample minibatch of (query, positive) pin pairs
                 -> random-walk neighbourhoods (top-T by visit count)
                 -> gather features (visual, text, ...)  ---+
                                                            v
  GPU consumer:  K=2 conv layers; importance pooling (weights =
                 normalised visit counts); max-margin loss with
                 random + curriculum "hard" negatives
                                                            |
            INFERENCE (MapReduce, no repeated computation)  v
  layer-1 embeddings for all nodes -> join -> layer-2 embeddings
                  -> pin embeddings -> ANN index (related pins, homefeed)

4. Twitter/X: a full graph recommendation stack

 unified-user-actions / event bus (fav, RT, follow, reply, click ...)
        |                 |                          |
        v                 v                          v
  Recos-Injector     SimClusters Heron jobs     batch (Scalding/GCP)
  (filter, publish   (tweet embeddings updated   KnownFor, InterestedIn,
   per-service Kafka  on every fav)              producer/topic embeds,
   topics)                |                      TwHIN, RealGraph,
        |                 v                      Tweepcred PageRank
        v           SimClusters-ANN                    |
  GraphJet services  (cluster->top tweets index)       v
  UTEG UTG UUG UVG        |                  Representation Manager
        |                 |                  / Representation Scorer
        +--------+--------+-------------------------+
                 v
          CR-Mixer / tweet-mixer (fetch, mix, filter, light rank)
                 v
          Home Mixer -> heavy ranker -> For You timeline

4.3 SimClusters: sparse community embeddings

 source embedding SV (top N=50 clusters)
      |  fetch top M=400 tweets per cluster   (cluster->tweet index)
      v
 sparse approx matrix R (M*N tweets x N clusters)
      |  score = R . SV^T  (dot product on truncated vectors)
      v
 top X=200 tweets -> fetch full tweet embeddings (only ~6% of M*N)
      |  exact cosine vs SV, keep top Y above threshold Z
      v
 candidates (cosine >= ~0.7 treated as highly related in prod)

Architecture and techniques (as reported in [19])

 heterogeneous member/job/post/ad/company graph (multi-edge-type, timestamps)
        |
        v
 graph densification for cold start (add edges from low-degree nodes
        to similar/content-matched nodes)
        |
        v
 GNN training: temporal neighbourhood sampling (multi-hop),
   learnable ID embeddings + content features, long-term losses,
   adaptive neighbour sampling, grouped/sliced batches,
   shared-memory sampling queue, local gradient optimisation
        |
        v
 embeddings refreshed (batch + nearline) -> feature store / ANN
        |
        v
 downstream rankers & retrievers: Feed, Jobs, PYMK, Ads

Design (as reported in [20])

 orders (per city) -> user-dish / user-restaurant weighted bipartite graphs
        -> GraphSAGE (2-hop sampled, max-margin + low-rank positives)
        -> user, dish, restaurant embeddings (daily)
        -> similarity features -> ranking model -> Eats homefeed carousels

7.2 AliGraph / Graph-Learn and the Dynamic Graph Service

 graph updates (stream/bulk)
    -> Data Loader (build Vertex/EdgeUpdateRecord, partition)
    -> Sampling Workers (decompose n-hop query into 1-hop samplers,
       Sample Store + Subscription Table, Subgraph Router)
    -> Serving Workers (all n-hop samples for a vertex co-located;
       SubGraph Store)                      <- client request (Java GSL)
    -> sampled subgraph -> TF model service -> prediction
 (all hops connected via fault-tolerant queues)

08. Feature Engineering and Feature Stores: Point-in-Time Correctness, Streaming Features, and Serving-Time Logging

Architecture.

                 client events (Kafka "julep" client-event topics)
                         |                              |
          daily DataRecord dumps (HDFS)        Storm/Heron (summingbird)
                         |                              |
   summingbird-scalding batch job          real-time aggregate job
   (DataRecordAggregationMonoid)           (same AggregateGroup config)
                         |                              |
        offline aggregate store (HDFS)         memcached (RTA cache)
                         |                              |
            upload -> Manhattan KV                      |
                         \______________  ______________/
                                        \/
                    Home Mixer / pushservice feature hydration
                         (~6000 features per ranked candidate)

Architecture (as publicly described).

  Offline (batch)                           Online / near-real-time
  ----------------                          -----------------------
  HDFS / Hive data lake                     Kafka event streams
        |                                         |
  Spark / Hive SQL jobs                     streaming jobs (Samza, later Flink)
        |                                         |
  Feature tables (Hive) --- batch upload ---> Cassandra online feature store
        |                                         |
  training data generation               prediction service fetches features
        \___________________   ___________________/
                            \ /
               Feature DSL (select + transform, applied
                identically at training and serving)

5.1 Data model

v1 = GroupBy(
    sources=[Source(events=EventSource(table="data.purchases",
                                       topic="events/purchases",
                                       query=Query(selects=..., time_column="ts")))],
    keys=["user_id"], online=True,
    aggregations=[Aggregation(input_column="price", operation=Operation.SUM,
                              windows=[Window(d, TimeUnit.DAYS) for d in [3, 14, 30]]),
                  Aggregation(input_column="price", operation=Operation.LAST_K(10))])

5.3 The backfill algorithm: from quadratic joins to sawtooth windows

 window W, hop h:
 |<------------- tail: precomputed per hop bucket ------------->|<- head ->|
 [round(ts - W, h) ............................... round(ts, h)) [.. ts)
   shared by all queries whose ts falls in the same h-bucket      exact, per query

5.4 Online architecture: untiled vs tiled (Flink)

 Kafka -> Flink: [Source] -> [Spark-SQL eval (CatalystUtil)] -> [Window/tiling op]
                                                                  |  IRs per tile,
                                                                  v  emitted per event
                                         [Avro encode] -> [async KV sink]
 Fetcher: get(key, tiles in [now-W, now)) -> merge IRs -> finalize -> feature values
 e.g. COUNT/LAST over 3h, 1h tiles: [2,"B"] + [9,"A"] + [3,"C"] => [14, "C"]

6. LinkedIn: Feathr (and Venice as the serving substrate)

  HDFS / Spark (offline features) ----bulk push----\
                                                    >---> Venice (derived data store)
  Kafka -> Samza (nearline features) ---stream-----/        |  remote reads or
                                                             |  Da Vinci in-process cache
  Feathr definitions (anchors, derived, registry)            v
        |-- offline: PIT join onto observation data --> training sets
        `-- online: feature fetch for inference <------ ranking services

12.1 Serving-time feature logging ("log what you serve")

 request --> retrieval --> feature fetch (KV + request ctx + on-demand fns)
                              |                         |
                              |                   ranker scores
                              v                         v
                 log {request_id, ts, keys,      impressions shown
                      feature_vector, model_ver}        |
                              |                         v
                              |             engagement events (Kafka)
                              v                         |
                   warehouse: served_features     labels (delayed join
                              \                    with attribution window)
                               \_______  __________/
                                       \/
                     training rows = logged features + labels
                     (+ bootstrap/backfill only for NEW features via PITC)

09. Training Infrastructure at Scale: Sparse Embeddings, Sharding, Parameter Servers and Online Learning

1.1 Model anatomy

            dense features            sparse features (multi-hot IDs)
                 |                      |        |        |
            +---------+             +------+ +------+ +------+
            | bottom  |             | EMB  | | EMB  | | EMB  |   <- 99%+ of params
            |  MLP    |             | T1   | | T2   | | Tk   |      (sharded)
            +---------+             +------+ +------+ +------+
                 |                      | pool   | pool   | pool
                 +----------+-----------+--------+--------+
                            |  feature interaction (dot / cat)
                       +---------+
                       | top MLP |   <- small, data-parallel
                       +---------+
                            |
                         p(click)

1.2 Why it is hard to train efficiently

 forward:  sample-sharded batch -> [input dist: all-to-all of IDs]
           -> table-sharded lookup + pooling
           -> [output dist: all-to-all of pooled embeddings]
           -> data-parallel interaction + MLP on each GPU
 backward: MLP grads -> all-reduce (dense)
           pooled-emb grads -> all-to-all back to owners
           -> fused sparse optimizer update in place on owner GPU

2.3 TorchRec and FBGEMM_GPU

TorchRec TrainPipelineSparseDist (conceptual)
 time ->
 batch i   : [H2D copy][ID a2a][lookup][emb a2a][fwd/bwd MLP][emb grad a2a+update]
 batch i+1 :           [H2D copy][ID a2a]......(overlapped with batch i compute)
 batch i+2 :                     [H2D copy]....

3.3 Real-time (online) training pipeline

 user actions (clicks, plays, ...) ---> Kafka (action log)
 serving features ------------------>  Kafka (feature log)
                                           |
                                    Flink online joiner
                     (join by request key, in-memory cache + on-disk KV
                      for late actions; negative sampling)
                                           |
                     +---------------------+-------------------+
                     |                                         |
           Kafka (training examples)                   HDFS (batch dumps)
                     |                                         |
            online training workers  <--- same model --->  batch training
                     |
            training parameter servers (Cuckoo hash tables)
                     |  periodic sync: sparse incremental (minutes), dense (slower)
                     v
            serving parameter servers -> online serving

4.2 Kuaishou PERSIA: hybrid synchronous/asynchronous training

   data loaders --> embedding workers <--> embedding parameter servers (CPU, huge)
                          |   (async: get/put embeddings, stale-tolerant)
                          v
                  NN workers (GPU) -- sync all-reduce of dense grads (Bagua)

6.1 Patterns

  stream (Kafka) -> joiner (Flink/Storm-like) -> online trainer
       |                                            |
       |                          incremental export (delta of touched IDs)
       v                                            v
  offline log (HDFS) -> periodic full retrain   serving PS / embedding cache
                         (bootstrap / recovery)

10. Online Serving and Inference: Latency Budgets, Model Servers, Caches and Cascades

10.1 The shape of the problem: funnels and latency budgets

 corpus (10^6 - 10^10 items)
   |
   |  RETRIEVAL / candidate generation   ANN, inverted index, graph walks, in-mem
   v                                     follow-graph stores; cost ~O(log N) per source
 10^3 - 10^4 candidates
   |
   |  FEATURE HYDRATION                  fan-out to feature stores, embedding servers,
   v                                     counters; often the latency long pole
   |
   |  LIGHT / EARLY-STAGE RANKING        LR, GBDT, two-tower dot product, distilled nets
   v
 10^2 - 10^3 candidates
   |
   |  HEAVY RANKING                      large MTML DNN / transformer, GPU or accelerator
   v
 10^1 - 10^2 candidates
   |
   |  RE-RANKING / POLICY                diversity (DPP, author decay), filters, ads mix
   v
 response (page of K items)

10.2.1 Architecture (2023 Twitter stack)

                       +-------------------------------+
  client request ----> | Home Mixer (Product Mixer,     |
                       | Scala)                         |
                       +---+-----------+-----------+---+
                           |           |           |
              in-network   |   OON     |  graph     |  follow recs
           +---------------+  +--------+------+  +--+------------------+
           | Earlybird     |  | CR-Mixer /    |  | UTEG (GraphJet,     |
           | search index  |  | tweet-mixer   |  | in-memory graph)    |
           | + LR light    |  | + light rank  |  | FRS                 |
           |   ranker      |  | + result cache|  |                     |
           +-------+-------+  +-------+-------+  +----------+----------+
                   \                  |                    /
                    v                 v                   v
              +-------------------------------------------------+
              | Feature hydration (~6000 features): user-signal |
              | service, graph-feature-service, real-graph,     |
              | representation-manager (SimClusters/TwHIN emb.) |
              +--------------------------+----------------------+
                                         v
              +-------------------------------------------------+
              | Heavy ranker (parallel MaskNet, multi-task)     |
              | served by Navi (Rust; TF / ONNX runtimes)       |
              +--------------------------+----------------------+
                                         v
              filters / heuristics / visibility / ads + WTF mixing

10.3.1 Instagram Explore (2023 post)

  sources: two-tower retrieval, user-interaction histories, heuristics
            |   (item embeddings precomputed offline, ANN at request time)
            v
  first-stage ranking: lightweight two-tower-style model, trained partly to
            |          reproduce the late-stage ranker's output (distillation)
            v
  second-stage ranking: multi-task multi-label (MTML) DNN predicting many
            |           engagement events
            v
  value model: weighted combination of MTML outputs (weights tuned online)
            |
            v
  final re-ranking: integrity filters, diversity / business rules

10.5.1 Vespa phased ranking

               query (user vector, filters, terms)
                              |
                    +---------v---------+
                    | stateless         |  scatter
                    | container         |-----------+-------------+
                    +---------^---------+           |             |
                              | gather         +----v----+   +----v----+
                              |                | content |   | content |
                              |                | node 1  |   | node N  |
                              |                |         |   |         |
                              |                | match:  |   | match:  |
                              |                | WAND /  |   | WAND /  |
                              |                | ANN     |   | ANN     |
                              |                | 1st ph. |   | 1st ph. |
                              |                | (all    |   | (all    |
                              |                |  hits)  |   |  hits)  |
                              |                | 2nd ph. |   | 2nd ph. |
                              |                | (top    |   | (top    |
                              |                |  ~100)  |   |  ~100)  |
                              |                +----+----+   +----+----+
                              +---------------------+-------------+
                    global-phase (top ~100 merged, ONNX, optional GPU)

10.6 Generative recommenders at serving time: HSTU and M-FALCON

 naive: for each candidate c_j:  encode([history ; c_j])     cost ~ m * O(n^2)

 M-FALCON:
   encode history once ---------------> cached keys/values (n tokens)
   split m candidates into microbatches of size b
   for each microbatch: attend [b candidates] -> cached history,
                        candidates masked from each other
                                                              cost ~ O(n^2) + m*O(n)

10.7.2 NVIDIA Merlin HugeCTR Hierarchical Parameter Server

  request keys
      |
      v
  +-------------------+   hit (hot IDs, majority of lookups under skew)
  | GPU embedding     |------------------------------------------> GPU dense model
  | cache (HBM)       |
  +---------+---------+
            | miss
            v
  +-------------------+   CPU-memory tier (e.g. in-process hash map / Redis cluster)
  | volatile DB tier  |
  +---------+---------+
            | miss
            v
  +-------------------+   SSD-backed persistent tier (e.g. RocksDB), full table
  | persistent tier   |
  +-------------------+
      ^
      | streaming updates from training (online model refresh)

6.2 Training/serving skew and leakage

 LEAKAGE TIMELINE (what a point-in-time join must enforce)

   feature_ts <= impression_ts  <  label_ts <= impression_ts + attribution_window
   |------- allowed features ---|-- label collection --|
                                ^ impression            ^ label final
   Using any feature value with ts > impression_ts = leakage.

                                     v
                       DPP workers (CPU fleet: read cols, decode,
                       transform, batch)  ==tensors==>  GPU trainers

 ONLINE                                 NEARLINE                       OFFLINE
 ------                                 --------                       -------

  req_id, user_id, ts, model_ver,       - key by req_id/imp_id         - re-join with full
  [item_id, position, features,       - short window (min-hours)        attribution window
