22.3.2 Microsoft Research: the GraphRAG family

 GraphRAG (eager)                         LazyGraphRAG (deferred)
 docs -> LLM entity/rel extraction        docs -> noun-phrase extraction (NLP)
      -> graph -> Leiden communities           -> concept graph -> communities
      -> LLM community summaries          query -> LLM expands to 3-5 subqueries
 query -> map over summaries                    -> best-first chunk ranking
       -> reduce -> answer                      -> LLM relevance tests (budget)
                                                -> map claims -> reduce answer
 cost paid at index time                  cost paid at query time, tunable

23. Canonical RAG and Retrieval Paper Reading List: 100+ Papers (2017-2026) by Theme, with Venue, Link and System-Design Takeaway

Overview

                         OFFLINE (indexing)
  docs --> parse --> chunk/propositions --> embed ------> ANN index (HNSW/DiskANN/IVF)
             |        (Dense X, late      (DPR, E5,     (HNSW, FAISS, ScaNN, SPANN)
             |         chunking, RAPTOR,   ColBERT,
             |         contextual retr.)   SPLADE)  \--> sparse index (BM25/SPLADE)
             \--> entity/relation extraction --> graph + communities (GraphRAG, HippoRAG)

                         ONLINE (query time)
  query --> rewrite/expand --> retrieve (hybrid) --> rerank --> compress --> generate
            (HyDE, Query2doc,   (RRF fusion)     (monoT5,   (RECOMP,     (FiD, RAG,
             step-back)                           RankGPT)   LLMLingua)   Self-RAG)
     ^                                                                        |
     |____ iterate / route / abstain (IRCoT, FLARE, CRAG, Search-R1, CoRAG) __|

  serving: KV reuse (RAGCache, CacheBlend), drafting (Speculative RAG)
  evaluation: BEIR, MTEB, KILT, RAGAS, ARES, RGB, BRIGHT, FRAMES, TREC RAG

2. Retrievers: dense, sparse, late-interaction and general embedders

  single-vector bi-encoder     late interaction (ColBERT)      learned sparse (SPLADE)
  q -> [v]  d -> [v]           q -> [v1..vn] d -> [u1..um]     q,d -> |V|-dim sparse
  score = <v_q, v_d>           score = sum_i max_j <vi,uj>     score = <w_q, w_d>
  1 vector / passage           1 vector / token (compressed)   inverted index, BM25-like
  cheapest, weakest OOD        best quality/storage trade-off  exact-match + expansion

6. Indexing granularity, chunking and hierarchy

  granularity spectrum
  proposition --- sentence --- chunk (100-800 tok) --- section --- doc --- summary tree
  (Dense X)                   (+context prefix:                   (LongRAG) (RAPTOR)
                               Anthropic contextual retrieval;
                               +late chunking: embed whole doc,
                               pool per chunk)

7. Iterative, adaptive and corrective retrieval

  ReAct / IRCoT style loop                    Self-RAG / CRAG style gates
  +---------+  thought   +----------+         retrieve? --no--> generate
  |  LLM    |----------->| search   |            | yes
  | reason  |<-----------| tool     |         retrieve -> grade docs
  +---------+  evidence  +----------+            | correct    | ambiguous | incorrect
      | stop when answer is supported         refine     refine+web    web search
      v                                          \____________|___________/
   answer (+ citations)                                generate -> critique (IsSup)

8. RL-trained search agents and deep research (2025-2026)

  rollout:  <think> ... </think> <search> q1 </search> <information> d1..dk </information>
            <think> ... </think> <search> q2 </search> <information> ...  </information>
            <answer> a </answer>
  reward:   rule-based outcome match(a, gold)       (no process reward)
  trick:    mask retrieved tokens from the policy-gradient loss (not model-generated)
  infra:    retriever served as an HTTP tool (e5 + Wikipedia in Search-R1's quick start)

9. Graph and structured RAG

  GraphRAG (Microsoft)                            HippoRAG
  chunks -> LLM entity/relation extraction        chunks -> OpenIE triples -> KG
        -> KG -> hierarchical Leiden communities   query -> entities -> seed nodes
        -> LLM community summaries (C0..C3)        Personalized PageRank over KG
  global query: map over summaries -> reduce       -> passage scores -> top-k passages
  cost: LLM pass over the whole corpus at index    cost: OpenIE pass, cheap queries

11. Compression, caching and serving efficiency

  standard prefix cache:  [sys][docA][docB][q]  hit only if identical prefix
  RAGCache:               knowledge tree of doc KV states across GPU/host memory,
                          prefix-aware eviction, overlap retrieval with prefill
  CacheBlend:             reuse non-prefix chunk KV caches, recompute a small subset
                          of tokens to restore cross-attention; overlap with KV load

A suggested reading order (two weeks)

  week 1  (core)                          week 2  (frontier)
  d1  DrQA [1], DPR [4], RAG [5]          d6  Self-RAG [53], CRAG [54], Adaptive-RAG [55]
  d2  FiD [6], RETRO [8], In-Ctx RALM[10] d7  Search-o1 [58], Search-R1 [59], CoRAG [57]
  d3  ColBERTv2 [14], SPLADE [17],        d8  GraphRAG [67], LazyGraphRAG [70],
      HNSW [27], DiskANN [31]                 HippoRAG 2 [73]
  d4  monoT5 [34], RankGPT [35],          d9  RAGCache [88], CacheBlend [89],
      HyDE [37], contextual retr. [46]        Speculative RAG [56], ColPali [92]
  d5  Lost in Middle [77], Power of       d10 BEIR [96], BRIGHT [98], RAGAS [100],
      Noise [78], Sufficient Ctx [83]         ARES [101], Seven Failure Points [107]

  | stuff prompt -> answer      |      | hybrid + domain retriever + reranker         |
  |                             |      | metadata scoping (company, year, jurisdiction)|
  |                             |      | evidence extraction / summarization          |

  |                             |      | domain benchmark + expert review             |
  +-----------------------------+      +---------------------------------------------+

1.4 Clinical-guideline RAG: a recurring pattern

  guideline PDFs (versioned, dated, issuing body)
        |
        v
  structure-aware parsing: recommendation units, strength-of-evidence grade,
  population/intervention tags, tables & flowcharts -> text
        |
        v
  index: hybrid (BM25 + dense) + metadata {body, version, date, population}
        |
  query --> concept normalization (UMLS CUIs / MeSH) --> filtered retrieval
        |
        v
  answer that quotes recommendation text + grade + guideline/version citation
        |
        v
  abstain / escalate if no recommendation covers the population

1.5 Ontology- and graph-aware medical retrieval: UMLS, MeSH, SPOKE

  Tier 1 (top)    : private user data (e.g., MIMIC-IV notes) -> LLM-extracted graph
                          |  "trinity" links
  Tier 2 (middle) : medical books + papers (MedC-K, PubMed-linked S2ORC) -> graph
                          |
  Tier 3 (bottom) : medical dictionary (UMLS) -- already graph-structured
  Retrieval walks top -> down so every patient-level claim can be grounded
  in literature and finally in a controlled-vocabulary definition.

2.2 LegalBench and LegalBench-RAG

 query: "Does the NDA allow the receiving party to share with consultants?"
     |
     v
 retriever under test --> returned spans [(file, start, end), ...]
     |
     v
 overlap with gold spans at CHARACTER granularity
     -> precision@k = chars(returned ∩ gold) / chars(returned)
     -> recall@k    = chars(returned ∩ gold) / chars(gold)

        |

  secondary sources (treatises, practice notes) + editorial metadata
  (headnotes, key numbers / topic taxonomies, citator status)

3.1 FinanceBench

   Configuration                          Retrieval scope        Outcome (qualitative)
   ------------------------------------   --------------------   -------------------------
   closed book                            none                   mostly wrong / refuse
   shared vector store (all filings)      corpus-wide top-k      81% wrong/refuse (GPT-4T)
   single-document vector store           the right filing       better
   long context (whole filing in prompt)  entire document        better; slow, limited
   oracle (gold evidence page)            perfect                upper bound

3.2 SEC-filing pipeline design

  EDGAR (full-text search / submissions / XBRL APIs)
        |
        +--> XBRL facts (us-gaap tags, units, periods) --> SQL/columnar store
        |                                                     ^
        +--> filing HTML/PDF --> section parser (Item 1A Risk, Item 7 MD&A, Notes)
                    |                table extractor (keep row/col headers + units)
                    v
             chunks with metadata {CIK, ticker, form, fiscal_year, period, section}
                    |
  query --> entity+period resolver (ticker, "FY23", "last quarter") --> HARD filter
        --> router: numeric? -> XBRL/SQL tool  |  narrative? -> hybrid retrieval
        --> calculator / code tool for ratios, growth, sums
        --> answer with citation (form, period, section, page) + computed trace

4.1 PaperQA and PaperQA2 (FutureHouse)

                       +------------------ agent LLM (ToolSelector) ------------------+
 question ----------->  |  chooses tools in any order, within budget/timeouts           |
                       +--------------------------------------------------------------+
         |                         |                          |                   |
         v                         v                          v                   v
  [paper_search]           [gather_evidence]          [citation traversal]  [gen_answer]
  LLM writes keyword       embed query, top-k         follow refs/citers    best summaries
  queries -> metadata      chunks (evidence_k=10)     of good papers        (<=5 sources)
  providers (Semantic      -> per-chunk, query-       (internal versions; -> cited answer
  Scholar, Crossref,       specific SUMMARY + score    not in OSS repo)   or "insufficient
  Unpaywall, OpenAlex)     -> LLM re-score/select                            information"
  -> fetch PDFs, chunk,       = "RCS": rerank +
     embed, add to state      contextual summarization

Architecture.

  query
    |
    v
  bi-encoder retriever (Contriever continued-pretrained on peS2o)
    |  over OpenScholar Datastore: ~45M open-access papers, ~236M passage embeddings
    |  (+ optional Semantic Scholar API and web search)
    v
  cross-encoder reranker (trained from BGE-reranker-large)
    |
    v
  generator: OpenScholar-8B (Llama 3.1 8B, ~13k instruction examples)
    |
    v
  SELF-FEEDBACK LOOP: model critiques its draft ("missing evidence on X",
    "citation unsupported") -> issues new retrievals -> revises
    |
    v
  citation verification pass -> final answer with per-sentence citations

4.3 Ai2 ScholarQA

 1. Query decomposition (LLM): rewrite query; extract filters (year, venue,
    field of study); produce keyword-search variant
 2. Retrieval: Semantic Scholar snippet search over ~11M+ full texts
    and ~100M+ abstracts; keyword search adds abstracts
 3. Rerank: cross-encoder (mxbai-rerank-large-v1); aggregate passages to PAPER level
 4. Quote extraction (LLM): pull exact supporting quotes per paper
 5. Outline + clustering (LLM): plan sections, choose format (paragraph vs list),
    assign quotes to sections
 6. Section generation (LLM): write each section from its quotes + prior sections,
    inline citations to quotes
 7. Literature-review tables for list-type sections (ArxivDIGESTables method)

5.2 Ontology-aware retrieval

  raw query --> NER --> normalize to IDs ------------------------------+
                       medicine: UMLS CUI / MeSH descriptor / RxNorm   |
                       law:      citation parser, key-number/topic     |
                       finance:  ticker->CIK, XBRL us-gaap concept     |
                       science:  S2 paper IDs, field-of-study, DOIs    |
                                                                       v
       expansion (synonyms, children)  +  hard filters  +  graph neighbors
                                       |
                                       v
                    hybrid retrieval (BM25 on expanded terms + dense)

25. Generative Retrieval and Model-Based Indexing: DSI, NCI, SEAL, TIGER, CorpusBrain and Their Hybrids

1.2 Canonical architecture

                  OFFLINE                                  ONLINE
 +-------------+    +-----------------+          +-----------+
 | corpus D    |--->| docid assignment|          |  query q  |
 +-------------+    | (atomic / k-means|          +-----+-----+
        |           |  / RQ / title /  |                |
        |           |  n-gram / terms) |                v
        |           +--------+--------+       +-------------------+
        |                    |                |  seq2seq / decoder |
        v                    v                |  LM (weights hold  |
 +-------------+    +-----------------+       |  the "index")      |
 | doc2query   |--->| training pairs  |------>+---------+---------+
 | (synthetic  |    | doc->id, q->id  |  train          | logits
 |  queries)   |    +-----------------+                 v
 +-------------+                              +-------------------+
                    +-----------------+       | constrained beam   |
                    | constraint store|------>| search (prefix trie|
                    | trie / FM-index |       | or FM-index)       |
                    +-----------------+       +---------+---------+
                                                        | top-k docids
                                                        v
                                              +-------------------+
                                              | docid -> doc store |
                                              | (+ optional rerank)|
                                              +-------------------+

3.1 Architecture

 doc --BERT--> embedding --hierarchical k-means--> semantic docid  (e.g. 3-7-1-4)
 doc --docT5query--> synthetic queries --+
 real queries ---------------------------+--> T5 encoder
                                                    |
                                    PAWA decoder: prefix-aware,
                                    weight-adaptive output layer
                                    (same digit has different
                                    embedding at each position/prefix)
                                                    |
                                   consistency regularization
                                   (two dropout passes must agree)
                                                    |
                                   constrained beam search -> docids

4.2 Architecture

 query --> BART-large --> constrained decoding over FM-index
                               |
             FM-index (compressed suffix array over all passages):
             - next-token lookup: which tokens can follow prefix p?
             - occurrence count: how many times does p appear?
             - locate: which documents contain p?
                               |
               generated n-grams {g1, g2, ...} with LM scores
                               |
          document score = aggregate over n-grams it contains
          (LM prob x corpus-frequency-based weight; intersective
           scoring combines multiple non-overlapping n-grams)
                               |
                        ranked passages

5.2 Architecture

  item content (title, brand, category, description)
            |
     sentence encoder (e.g. Sentence-T5)  -> dense item vector x
            |
     RQ-VAE: level 1 codebook -> c1, residual r1 = x - e(c1)
             level 2 codebook -> c2, residual r2 = r1 - e(c2)
             level 3 codebook -> c3
             (+ extra token to disambiguate collisions)
            |
     semantic ID = (c1, c2, c3, c4)   e.g. (12, 201, 7, 0)
            |
  user history: [SID(item_1)] [SID(item_2)] ... [SID(item_n)]
            |
     Transformer encoder-decoder  --> autoregressively decode SID(item_n+1)
            |
     constrained to valid SIDs (prefix trie)  -> next items

6.2 Architecture and pre-training

  Wikipedia page P  --sample sentence / lead para / anchor ctx-->  pseudo-query
                                                                       |
                                   BART-large  <---- pretrain: pseudo-query -> title(P)
                                       |
                    fine-tune (few or many labels) on KILT tasks
                                       |
          query --> constrained beam search over KILT title trie --> pages

7.3 Closing the gap: RIPOR and PAG (UMass, 2024)

 phase 1: train T5 as a dense encoder (relevance-based)
 phase 2: residual-quantize its doc vectors -> RQ docids (relevance-aware prefixes)
 phase 3: seq2seq training with prefix-oriented ranking loss:
          at every prefix length, rank relevant docid prefixes above negatives
          mined from constrained beam search, + dense-retriever training data

9.1 GR as a coarse router over a vector index

query --> small seq2seq --> beam over coarse codes (c1,c2) --> top clusters
                                                              |
query --> dense encoder ------------------------------------> ANN within clusters
                                                              |
                                                     merged candidates --> reranker

9.3 GR plus LLM rerankers (2024-2026)

                 +--> dense ANN (bi-encoder) -----------+
query -----------+                                       +--> fuse (RRF) --> LLM reranker
                 +--> generative retriever (beam k) ----+          |
                 |    (titles / RQ codes / n-grams)                v
                 +--> BM25 / SPLADE --------------------+     top-n ctx --> LLM answer

5.1 Tool search tool and defer_loading

 request.tools = [ tool_search_tool_{regex|bm25}_20251119,      <- always loaded
                   hot_tool_1 .. hot_tool_k,                     <- defer_loading: false
                   tool_1 .. tool_N (N <= 10,000)                <- defer_loading: true ]

 +-------------------- Anthropic API (server side) ---------------------+
 |  system-prompt prefix = search tool + hot tools   (cacheable)        |
 |                                                                      |
 |  Claude --server_tool_use(pattern|query, limit)--> search over       |
 |          names, descriptions, arg names, arg descriptions            |
 |        <--tool_search_tool_result: [tool_reference x <=5 default]--  |
 |                                                                      |
 |  API expands each tool_reference inline into the full definition     |
 |  (prefix untouched -> prompt cache preserved)                        |
 |                                                                      |
 |  Claude --tool_use(discovered_tool)--> client executes, returns      |
 +----------------------------------------------------------------------+

Retriever choice.

              +------------------- catalog build (offline) -------------------+
 MCP servers->| normalize schema -> tool doc text -> +synthetic queries        |
 OpenAPI    ->| server summary  -> embed(server), embed(tool), BM25 index     |
 functions  ->| dedupe / merge near-duplicate tools                           |
              +------------------------------+--------------------------------+
                                             |
              +-------------- runtime (per turn) -----------------------------+
              | context: system + hot tools + search tool  (cached prefix)    |
              | model -> search(query) -> hybrid top-50 -> rerank -> top-5    |
              |       <- tool_reference x5 (expanded inline, not in prefix)   |
              | model -> tool_use -> result -> may search again               |
              | log: query, returned ids, chosen id, success -> eval set      |
              +---------------------------------------------------------------+

27. Multilingual and Cross-Lingual RAG: Benchmarks, Embeddings, Language Bias, Tokenization and Sovereign Deployments

Overview

             THE MULTILINGUAL RAG DESIGN SPACE
 +-----------+   +-------------------+   +-----------------+   +-------------+
 | Query     |   | Retrieval         |   | Context         |   | Generation  |
 | (lang Lq) |-->| - translate q?    |-->| - which langs?  |-->| - prompt    |
 | script,   |   | - shared space?   |   | - translate     |   |   language  |
 | code-mix  |   | - per-lang index? |   |   passages?     |   | - answer in |
 +-----------+   | - hybrid lexical  |   | - token budget  |   |   Lq?       |
                 +-------------------+   +-----------------+   | - citations |
                          ^                                    +-------------+
                          |
          +-------------------------------+
          | Corpus (langs L1..Ln)         |
          | script-aware chunking,        |
          | per-language analyzers,       |
          | optional doc translation      |
          +-------------------------------+

Architectural choices: translate versus shared multilingual space

 (A) TRANSLATE-THEN-RETRIEVE (query translation, "English pivot")
     q(Lq) --MT--> q(en) --> [English index] --> passages(en) --> LLM --> answer(Lq)

 (B) DOCUMENT TRANSLATION (translate corpus offline)
     docs(L1..Ln) --MT offline--> docs(en) --> [English index]
     q(Lq) --MT--> q(en) --------------^      (keep originals for citation)

 (C) SHARED MULTILINGUAL SPACE
     q(Lq) --enc--> v_q --+
                          +--> [one index over docs(L1..Ln) encoded by same model]
     docs(Li) --enc--> v_d+       --> passages(mixed langs) --> LLM --> answer(Lq)

 (D) DISTILLED NEURAL CLIR (ColBERT-X / Translate-Distill)
     teacher: MT + English cross-encoder scores (q_en, d_en)
     student: multilingual late-interaction encoder scores (q_en, d_Li) directly

(C) Shared multilingual embedding space

          BGE-M3 ONE-PASS, THREE-SIGNAL RETRIEVAL
  text --> XLM-R-style encoder (8192 ctx) --> hidden states H
              |                 |                       |
       [CLS] -> dense      token weights -> sparse   all tokens -> multi-vec
       1024-d ANN index    inverted index           late interaction (rerank)
              \_________________|_______________________/
                     weighted score fusion --> cross-encoder rerank

Debiasing the "English is best" result (Park et al., 2026)

          DELTA-STYLE QUERY FUSION (monolingual alignment)
  q(ko) --+-- as is ------------------> search(ko docs)  --+
          +-- MT -> q(en) ------------> search(en docs)  --+--> fuse/rerank --> LLM
          +-- MT -> q(ja), q(zh) ... -> search(ja,zh docs)-+
          (each language partition is queried in its own language)

Consequences specific to RAG

  Same 5 passages, same information, different language
  English    [#####                ]  ~1.0x tokens
  Mid-res    [##########           ]  ~2x   (approximate, varies by tokenizer)
  Low-res    [####################+]  up to ~4-15x  [24]
  -> fewer passages fit in the budget, higher prefill cost, slower TTFT

Script-aware chunking pipeline

 raw doc --> Unicode NFC normalize --> language/script ID (per paragraph)
         --> per-script sentence segmenter
               CJK: punctuation rules     Thai: dictionary/model segmenter [27]
               Arabic/Persian: punctuation + ZWNJ-safe normalization
               Indic: danda + grapheme-cluster-safe boundaries
         --> pack sentences into chunks sized in TOKENS of the embedder,
             with per-language targets derived from the measured premium [24]
         --> attach title/heading prefix (BERGEN prefixes article title) [18]
         --> store: original text, lang tag, script tag, optional translation

Reference architecture: a sovereign multilingual RAG stack

                    REGION / JURISDICTION BOUNDARY (data residency)
 +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 |  Ingest                                                                 |
 |   docs --> NFC + lang/script ID --> script-aware chunker --> chunks     |
 |              |                                              (lang tags) |
 |              +--> optional MT to pivot (IndicTrans2 / NLLB) [18,28]      |
 |                                                                         |
 |  Index                                                                  |
 |   multilingual embedder (BGE-M3 / mE5 / regional embedder) [11,13,29]   |
 |   + per-language lexical or learned-sparse index                        |
 |   partitions by language for monolingual-alignment search [21]          |
 |                                                                         |
 |  Query                                                                  |
 |   q --> lang ID --> query variants per corpus language (MT, short) [21] |
 |     --> hybrid search per partition --> cross-lingual reranker          |
 |     --> token-budgeted packer (per-language premium) [24]               |
 |                                                                         |
 |  Generate                                                               |
 |   regional/open LLM (SEA-LION / Apertus / ...) [29,30]                  |
 |   prompt written in target language; answer + entities in Lq [18]      |
 |   abstain policy tested on NoMIRACL-style data [23]                     |
 |                                                                         |
 |  Evaluate                                                               |
 |   per-language: retrieval nDCG, char-3gram recall, correct-language     |
 |   rate, citation-language mix, hallucination/error rate [18,20,22,23]   |
 +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+

28. On-Device, Edge and Air-Gapped RAG: Small Models, Embedded Indexes and Private Compute

Overview

              ON-DEVICE / EDGE RAG REFERENCE STACK
 +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
 |  App / OS assistant (Siri, Recall, in-app chat, field tool)        |
 +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
 |  Orchestrator: query rewrite, tool routing, escalation policy      |
 +-----------------+--------------------------+-----------------------+
 |  SLM generator  |  Retriever               |  Escalation           |
 |  1-4B, 2-4 bit  |  lexical + vector (+KG)  |  verifiable cloud     |
 |  ~4K-8K ctx     |  rerank optional         |  (PCC-like) or none   |
 +-----------------+--------------------------+-----------------------+
 |  Embedded index: SQLite/vec0, Lance files, ObjectBox HNSW,         |
 |  OS index (Spotlight / AppContentIndexer); encrypted at rest       |
 +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
 |  Background indexer: OCR, chunking, small embedder (NPU/CPU),      |
 |  scheduled when charging / idle; incremental updates               |
 +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
 |  Hardware: shared DRAM (8-16 GB), NPU/GPU, flash, battery, TPM/SE  |
 +--------------------------------------------------------------------+

4.1 sqlite-vec

create virtual table vec_docs using vec0(
  doc_id integer primary key,
  user_id integer partition key,      -- shard per user / tenant
  embedding float[768],
  embedding_coarse bit[768]           -- 96 bytes per row
);
select doc_id, distance from vec_docs
 where embedding match :q and user_id = :u
 order by distance limit 10;

5.1 EdgeRAG (Seemakhupt, Liu, Khan, 2024)

           EdgeRAG retrieval path (after [1])
 query --> embed --> compare to centroids (resident) --> top-n clusters
                                                          |
             +--------------------------------------------+
             v
   cluster large?  --yes-->  load precomputed embeddings from storage
       | no
       v
   in cache?       --yes-->  use cached embeddings
       | no
       v
   re-embed cluster chunks on device --> insert into cost-aware cache
             |
             v
   exact search within clusters --> top-k chunks --> SLM generator

   entity-name vector index      type pool filter

                         v

                         |

          compact context (~500 tok node ctx) -> SLM answer

29. The Generation Side of RAG: Knowledge Conflicts, Robustness, Compression, Attribution, and Abstention

Overview

                    THE GENERATION-SIDE CONTROL SURFACE
 query ──► retriever ──► top-k passages
                              │
             ┌────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┐
             │ (A) context transforms                        │
             │  evaluate / filter (CRAG, NLI filter)         │
             │  compress (RECOMP, LongLLMLingua, xRAG)       │
             │  reorder (put strong evidence at the edges)   │
             └────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
                              ▼
             ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────┐
             │ (B) generator, trained for:                   │
             │  noise robustness (RetRobust, RAFT)           │
             │  explicit reading notes (Chain-of-Note,       │
             │    InstructRAG rationales)                    │
             │  self-critique tokens (Self-RAG)              │
             │  citation (FRONT, LongCite, SelfCite)         │
             │  refusal (Trust-Align)                        │
             └────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
                              ▼
             ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────┐
             │ (C) decoding controls                         │
             │  context-aware decoding (contrast with/without)│
             │  isolate-then-aggregate (RobustRAG)           │
             └────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
                              ▼
             ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────┐
             │ (D) release gate                              │
             │  sufficient-context check, citation verifier, │
             │  hallucination detector -> answer | abstain   │
             └───────────────────────────────────────────────┘

29.1.4 ConflictBank (NeurIPS 2024 Datasets and Benchmarks)

Wikidata facts ──► SLING fact extraction ──► (s, r, o) claims
                                              │
              LLM generates conflicting evidence per claim, by cause:
              ├─ misinformation conflict (false object)
              ├─ temporal conflict (fact changed over time)
              └─ semantic conflict (same surface form, different sense)
                                              │
              quality control: NLI (deberta-v3-base-tasksource-nli)
                             + embedding similarity (all-mpnet-base-v2)
                                              │
        7,453,853 claim-evidence pairs  +  553,117 QA pairs
                                              │
   optional: continual pre-training to embed conflicts into LLMs
   (LLaMA-Factory, 4,500 steps, batch 32/device, lr 2e-5 cosine,
    max length 4,096)

29.2.3 Chain-of-Note (Tencent AI Lab, 2023)

  passages p1..pk
       │
       ▼
  note(p1): "p1 directly answers -> X"            (pattern 1: direct answer)
  note(p2): "p2 is related context, implies X"     (pattern 2: inferred answer)
  note(p3): "p3 irrelevant"                       (pattern 3: none useful)
       │
       ▼
  final answer  OR  "unknown" when no note supports an answer

29.2.6 CRAG: Corrective RAG (2024)

              retrieval evaluator (fine-tuned, lightweight)
                 │            │               │
             Correct      Ambiguous       Incorrect
                 │            │               │
      decompose-then-    combine both   rewrite query ->
      recompose: split   branches       web search (serper.dev);
      into strips,                      prefer Wikipedia or all
      drop irrelevant
                 └────────────┴───────────────┘
                              ▼
                          generator

29.3.4 xRAG (NeurIPS 2024): one-token context

 document ──► retriever encoder (frozen; SFR-Embedding-Mistral) ──► e_doc
                                                                   │
                                   modality bridge (small trained projector)
                                                                   │
                                     one soft token in LLM embedding space
                                                                   │
 [instruction] [xRAG token] [question] ──► frozen LLM (Mistral-7B / Mixtral)

29.4.2 FRONT: fine-grained grounded citations (HIT, ACL 2024 Findings)

 Stage 1  Grounding-Guided Generation (SFT)
   query + passages ──► select supporting QUOTES (fine-grained spans)
                       ──► generate answer conditioned on quotes, cite them

 Stage 2  Consistency-Aware Alignment (DPO)
   sample answers ──► score consistency between grounded quotes and answer
                  ──► preference pairs ──► DPO

29.6.2 Sufficient context (Google, ICLR 2025)

 query + context ──► sufficiency autorater ──► s ∈ {sufficient, insufficient}
        │                                         │
        └──► generator ──► answer + confidence p  │
                                    │             │
                     selective policy: f(s, p) > τ ? answer : abstain
                     (τ tuned for a target coverage/accuracy point)

29.8 Reference Architecture: A Conflict-Aware, Attributed Generator

 query
   │
   ├─► closed-book pass (small budget) ─► prior answer a0, logprob p0 ───────┐
   │                                                                          │
   └─► retrieve top-k ─► CRAG-style evaluator ──(incorrect)──► rewrite+web    │
                              │ (correct / ambiguous)                         │
                              ▼                                               │
              strip-level filter / LongLLMLingua (question-aware,             │
              dynamic ratio, reorder strongest -> edges)                      │
                              │                                               │
                              ▼                                               │
     generator fine-tuned with distractors + rationales + quote-first        │
     citations + grounded refusal (RAFT / InstructRAG / FRONT / Trust-Align)  │
          optional: CAD (trusted sources only) or isolate-then-aggregate      │
          (untrusted / user-uploaded sources)                                 │
                              │                                               │
                              ▼                                               │
     answer a1 + citations {claim, doc, span}                                 │
                              │                                               │
     verifiers: exact-span check │ NLI recall/precision │ ablation reward     │
                              │                                               │
     conflict check: a0 != a1 and p0 high? ◄──────────────────────────────────┘
                              │
     release gate: sufficiency x confidence > τ ? answer : abstain / escalate

30. Algorithm-System Co-Design for RAG Inference: Overlap, Reuse, Compression, and Placement

Overview

            Where RAG latency comes from (one request, non-iterative)

 query --> [embed] --> [ANN search] --> [rerank] --> [prefill: Q + k docs] --> [decode]
             ~ms        10s ms to s      ~10s ms     dominant for long ctx   per token
                         (CPU/GPU/       (small LM)   O(L) FLOPs, O(L^2)     memory-bw
                          FPGA)                       attention               bound
   overlap targets:   PipeRAG, RaLMSpec, TeleRAG, Stream2LLM, RAGCache-DSP
   reuse targets:                                    RAGCache, CacheBlend, TurboRAG,
                                                     Block-Attn, CacheGen, LMCache
   shrink targets:                                   REFRAG, Speculative RAG
   placement targets: Chameleon, VectorLiteRAG, HedraRAG, RAGO (all stages)

Architecture

                   retrieved docs D (n docs)
                            |
            embed (instruction-aware encoder) + K-Means into k clusters
                            |
           sample 1 doc per cluster -> m subsets delta_1..delta_m (k docs each)
          /                 |                  \
   [Drafter 7B #1]   [Drafter 7B #2]  ...  [Drafter 7B #m]     (parallel endpoints)
     alpha_1, beta_1   alpha_2, beta_2        alpha_m, beta_m   (answer, rationale)
          \                 |                  /
           [Generalist verifier: one forward pass per draft, no docs in prompt]
            rho_j = rho_draft * rho_self-consistency * rho_self-reflection
                            |
                    answer = argmax_j rho_j

3.1 PipeRAG (Amazon; KDD 2025)

 time ->
 RETRO (serial):  [gen chunk i][retrieve i+1][gen chunk i+1][retrieve i+2] ...
 PipeRAG:         [gen chunk i      ][gen chunk i+1    ][gen chunk i+2 ] ...
                  [retrieve(stale q_i - s)][retrieve(stale)] ...  nprobe sized to fit

4. Reusing KV caches of retrieved knowledge

 prefix cache hit requires identical token prefix:
   req A: [sys][doc7][doc3][q_A]      req B: [sys][doc3][doc7][q_B]
           ^^^^^^^^^^^ hit for A only once [sys][doc7] cached; B misses on doc3
 non-prefix reuse (CacheBlend/TurboRAG/Block-Attn): reuse doc3 and doc7 KV anywhere,
   then repair cross-attention (selective recompute) or train model to not need it

4.1 RAGCache (Peking University and ByteDance; ACM TOCS)

                     root (system prompt)
                    /        |         \
                 doc12      doc3       doc40          <- GPU tier (hot)
                /    \        \
            doc3    doc8     doc12                    <- host tier (warm, swapped once)
            |
          doc8                                         path = ordered doc sequence

Architecture (Meta Superintelligence Labs, NUS and Rice; 2025)

 retrieved passages --> split into k-token chunks
        |
   [light encoder: RoBERTa-base/large]  -> 1 embedding per chunk   (query-independent,
        |                                                    precomputable/cacheable)
   [2-layer MLP projection] -> decoder token-embedding space
        |
   RL policy: which chunks to EXPAND back to full tokens (selective compression)
        |
 decoder input = [question tokens] + [chunk embeddings ... | expanded chunk tokens]
        |
   [LLaMA-2-7B decoder]  -> answer          input length shrinks ~k-fold

8.1 TTFT for one non-iterative request

TTFT ~= T_embed(q)
      + T_search(index, nprobe, placement)          # CPU / GPU / FPGA
      + T_rerank(k)                                  # small model, many tokens
      + T_kv_load(bytes_hit / BW_tier)               # 0 if no reuse; PCIe/NVMe/net
      + T_prefill((1 - h) * L_ctx + r * h * L_ctx + L_q)
      + T_queue
      - T_overlap                                    # hidden by pipelining
where
  L_ctx   = retrieved tokens (k chunks x chunk length)
  h       = fraction of context tokens with reusable KV (cache hit ratio)
  r       = recompute ratio on hits (0 for exact prefix; ~0.15 CacheBlend; 0 TurboRAG)
  T_overlap = min(T_search, T_pre-retrieval-LLM) (TeleRAG), or speculative prefill
              on partial results (RAGCache), or streaming chunks (Stream2LLM)

8.3 Throughput and cost per query

QPS ~= min( QPS_search(cores or GPU share, batch_s),
            QPS_prefill(GPU FLOPs, effective tokens),
            QPS_decode(HBM bandwidth, batch_d limited by free KV memory) )
cost/query ~= sum_over_resources( price_per_hour / 3600 / QPS_at_that_resource )

31. Real-Time and Time-Aware RAG: Streaming Ingestion, Temporal Retrieval and Staleness Evaluation

Overview

          THE THREE LAYERS OF TIME IN A RAG SYSTEM

  +-------------------+     +---------------------+     +-------------------+
  |  SOURCE SYSTEMS   |     |  INDEX / KNOWLEDGE  |     |  QUERY + ANSWER   |
  |  OLTP DB, docs,   | --> |  vectors, BM25,     | --> |  retrieve, rank,  |
  |  feeds, logs      |     |  temporal KG, vers. |     |  generate, cite   |
  +-------------------+     +---------------------+     +-------------------+
        |                           |                          |
   (1) INDEX FRESHNESS        (2) TEMPORAL MODEL         (3) TEMPORAL INTENT
   lag from commit to         valid-time vs              "now" vs "as of T"
   searchable                 transaction-time           recency vs relevance
        \___________________________|__________________________/
                                    |
                      (4) STALENESS EVALUATION
              index lag SLOs, fast-changing QA sets, canaries

2.1 The CDC pattern: Debezium + Kafka as the change log

   CDC -> STREAM -> EMBED -> VECTOR SINK (canonical streaming RAG ingestion)

  +-----------+  WAL/binlog  +-----------+   topic per table   +----------------+
  | Postgres  | -----------> | Debezium  | ------------------> | Kafka          |
  | (OLTP)    |              | (Connect) |   c/u/d events      | (ordered log)  |
  +-----------+              +-----------+                     +-------+--------+
                                                                       |
                         +---------------------------------------------+
                         v
              +------------------------+     +-------------------+
              | Stream processor       |     | Embedding service |
              | Flink / Pathway /      |<--->| (batched, cached, |
              | Bytewax: parse, chunk, |     |  rate-limited)    |
              | dedupe, key by doc_id  |     +-------------------+
              +-----------+------------+
                          | upsert(doc_id#chunk_i, vec, payload{ts,...})
                          | delete(doc_id#*) on tombstone
                          v
              +------------------------+       +----------------------+
              | Vector / hybrid index  | <---- | Query service (RAG)  |
              | Qdrant/Milvus/Mongo/ES |       | filters on ts fields |
              +------------------------+       +----------------------+

2.2 Flink-native RAG: Confluent Cloud streaming agents

     QUERIES-AS-STREAMS (Confluent quickstart Lab 2 shape)

  documents --> documents_embed --> [Mongo Atlas sink] --> vector index
                                                               ^
  queries ----> queries_embed ----> search_results ------------+ (vector search)
                                         |
                                         v
                              search_results_response  (LLM answer + snippets)

3.4 Temporal-intent gating

              TEMPORAL-INTENT-GATED RETRIEVAL

  query --> [temporal intent classifier] --+-- "timeless"  --> pure relevance
              (LLM prompt or small model)  |
                                           +-- "current"   --> relevance x decay(now)
                                           |                   + filter valid_to=NULL
                                           |
                                           +-- "as of T"   --> valid_from<=T<valid_to
                                           |                   decay centered on T
                                           +-- "range"     --> filter ts in [T1,T2]
                                                               (logs, events)

4.1 Temporal knowledge graphs: Zep / Graphiti

        BI-TEMPORAL FACT EDGE (Graphiti-style)

   (Alice) --[ROLE: VP Sales]--> (Acme)
        valid_from = 2021-03     valid_to = 2024-07      <- world time
        created_at = 2021-03-04  invalidated_at = 2024-07-15  <- system time
        episode_ref = slack#88213 (provenance)

   (Alice) --[ROLE: CRO]-------> (Acme)
        valid_from = 2024-07     valid_to = NULL (current)

   "Who runs sales now?"      -> edges with valid_to = NULL
   "Who ran sales in 2022?"   -> edges with valid_from <= 2022 < valid_to
   "What did we believe on 2024-07-10?" -> filter on created/invalidated (system time)

6.2 Event-stream and log RAG: observability copilots

        OBSERVABILITY COPILOT: TOOL-MEDIATED TEMPORAL RETRIEVAL

  user: "why did p99 latency spike after 14:00?"
     |
     v
  +--------------+  1. alert rules / incidents   +---------------------+
  |  LLM agent   | ----------------------------> | Grafana / Alerting  |
  |  (planner)   |  2. PromQL range [13:30,15:00]| Prometheus / Mimir  |
  |              | ----------------------------> |                     |
  |              |  3. Loki patterns, same range | Loki                |
  |              | ----------------------------> |                     |
  |              |  4. runbook / postmortem RAG  | vector KB (static)  |
  +------+-------+ ----------------------------> +---------------------+
         |
         v   answer = live telemetry (exact time range) + static knowledge (semantic)

8. Reference architecture

            REFERENCE: TIME-AWARE STREAMING RAG

  SOURCES             INGEST                    STORES                QUERY
  -------             ------                    ------                -----
  OLTP DB --CDC--+                        +-> vector/hybrid idx --+
  Docs/Drive ----+--> stream processor ---+   (valid_from/to,      |   +----------+
  News feeds ----+    chunk, dedupe,      |    published_at,       +-->| intent   |

                      (batch+retry)       +-> temporal KG ---------+   +----+-----+
                           |              |   (bi-temporal edges)  |        |
                           |              +-> versioned snapshot --+   timeless/now/
                           v                  (time travel, eval)  |   as-of/range
                    lag + backlog metrics                          |        |
                           |                LIVE TELEMETRY ---------+   +---v------+
                           v                (Prom/Loki/SIEM via     |   | retrieve |
                    freshness SLO alerts     tools, time-bounded)   +-->| + decay  |

32. Personalized and Conversational RAG: User Profiles, Per-User Scopes, Conversational Search, Voice Latency, Clarification and Shopping Assistants

Overview

               +-------------------- per-user scope (tenant / partition) -------+
               |  profile items   PTKB statements   memories   purchase history |
               +--------^-----------------^--------------^-------------^--------+
                        | profile retrieval (LaMP)      | (Ch. 18)    | recsys tools
 user turn t --> [context resolver] --> rewritten query + selected profile facts
   ^              (CAsT rewrite /          |
   |               ConvDR encoder)         v
   |                              [shared corpus retrieval] --> rerank --> generate
   |                                       |                                 |
   |   [clarify-or-answer policy] <--------+ (ambiguity / low confidence)    |
   +------------- clarifying question  <---+     cited answer / spoken reply <+

Architecture

  input x (e.g., article text)     user profile P_u = {d_1 ... d_n} (user's past docs)
          |                                   |
          +------> rank P_u by relevance to x: bm25 | contriever | recency
                                   |  (optional: prepend timestamps to items)
                                   v
                       top num_retrieved items --> prompt template --> LLM --> y_u

Reference baseline architecture (2023)

 context + utterance --> t5-base-canard rewriter (automatic runs)
                              |      or resolved_utterance (manual runs)
 PTKB --> cross-encoder ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2 scores each (query, statement) pair
                              |  top num_ptkb statements appended to rewritten query
                              v
                 BM25 (or QLD), optional RM3, k=100..1000 over ClueWeb22 subset
                              v
                 cross-encoder rerank --> top num_psg passages (default 3)
                              v
            t5-base-finetuned-summarize-news ("summarize: ..."), max 250 tokens
                              v
           response + passage provenance + PTKB provenance (official JSON)

OpenAI Realtime: preambles, supervisors and async tools

 user speech --> realtime model --(filler: "One moment...")--> audio out
                      | tool: getNextResponseFromSupervisor(relevantContext)
                      v
               text supervisor (gpt-4.1) --> lookupPolicyDocument / RAG / APIs
                      | message with citations   (~2 s in the README example)
                      v
               realtime model reads the answer aloud --> audio out

A clarify-or-retrieve policy

 turn --> resolve context --> ambiguity / missing-slot detector (ClariQ-style 1..4)
              |                               |
              | low need                      | high need and clarification budget left
              v                               v
       personalize from profile        ask ONE targeted question
       (fill slots from PTKB/memory)    (facets from retrieval results or a bank)
              v                               v
          retrieve + answer            user answer --> merge into query --> retrieve

Tool-based conversational recommendation: InteRecAgent

 user: "popular sports games like FIFA, not too pricey"
   LLM planner --> SQL tool (genre=sports, price<X) --> candidate set
              --> item-similarity tool (FIFA)      --> candidate set (merged)
              --> ranker(schema=preference, prefer=[...], unwanted=[...]) --> top-k
   LLM --> grounded recommendation with item facts from the profile table

8. The eval lifecycle

        +-------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |                                                                   |
        v                                                                   |
 [1] DEFINE SUCCESS ----> [2] LOOK AT DATA / ERROR ANALYSIS                 |
   task-specific criteria     read traces, label pass/fail,                 |
   edge cases, thresholds     discover criteria (criteria drift)            |
                                   |                                        |
                                   v                                        |
 [4] ALIGN GRADERS <------ [3] BUILD SUITE                                  |
   code first; LLM judge      20-50 real-failure tasks, balanced            |
   calibrated vs human        should/shouldn't cases, reference solns       |
   labels (EvalGen/AlignEval) unambiguous (2 experts agree)                 |
        |                                                                   |
        v                                                                   |
 [5] RUN & COMPARE -------> [6] GATE / DECIDE                               |
   trials, cache, CIs,        CI regression gate, release sign-off,         |
   paired diffs, read         safety threshold determination                |
   transcripts                     |                                        |
                                   v                                        |
 [8] HARVEST & GRADUATE <-- [7] DEPLOY & MONITOR ONLINE                     |
   prod failures -> new       sampled reference-free graders,               |
   offline cases; capability  user feedback, A/B tests,                     |
   evals at ~100% -> regress. annotation queues                             |
        |                                                                   |
        v                                                                   |
 [9] SATURATION REVIEW / RETIRE ---------------------------------------------+
   saturated -> harder tasks; stale/contaminated -> retire

02. The Benchmark Landscape Through 2026: How Frontier Benchmarks Are Built and Graded

Overview

             GRADING PRIMITIVES USED ACROSS THE 2023-2026 LANDSCAPE
+----------------------+-----------------------------+------------------------------+
| Primitive            | Examples                    | What can go wrong            |
+----------------------+-----------------------------+------------------------------+
| Letter/option match  | MMLU, MMLU-Pro, GPQA        | label noise, guessing,       |
|                      |                             | answer-extraction regex      |
| Exact grid / string  | ARC-AGI-1/2, AIME, MathArena| ambiguous items, formatting  |
| LLM-judge equivalence| HLE, BrowseComp             | judge errors, parser bugs    |
| Symbolic/scripted    | FrontierMath                | wrong reference answers      |
|   answer check       |                             |                              |
| Unit tests (fn-level)| HumanEval, LiveCodeBench    | weak tests, sandbox flakiness|
| Repo test suites     | SWE-bench family            | too-narrow / too-wide tests  |
| Env-state checks     | tau-bench, OSWorld, TB 2.0  | unreachable goals, flaky VMs |
| Efficiency vs humans | ARC-AGI-3 (RHAE)            | baseline choice, rule changes|
| Expert pairwise      | GDPval                      | cost, rater disagreement     |
| Human-time calibrated| METR time horizon           | small long-task sample       |
+----------------------+-----------------------------+------------------------------+

Overview

  author items --> adversarial filter --> expert review --> split
  (humans,          (drop items frontier    (correctness,     |  public / semi-private /
   scraped repos,    models already solve)   ambiguity)       |  private / commercial
   contests)                                                  v
                                       harness (prompt, tools, container, VM, budget)
                                                              |
                                                              v
                    grader (regex | judge LLM | tests | state hash | humans)
                                                              |
                                                              v
             aggregate (acc, pass@k, pass^k, RHAE, win rate, logistic horizon)
                                                              |
                                                              v
             post-release audit --> errata / v2 / retirement  (the stage most
                                                                designs underbudget)

1.3 GPQA: "Google-proof" by construction

  domain expert writes Q + 4 options + explanation
          |
          v
  expert validator #1 answers, gives feedback --> writer revises
          |
          v
  expert validator #2 answers (measures objectivity)
          |
          v
  3 non-expert validators (PhDs in OTHER fields), unrestricted web access
          |
          v
  subsets: Extended (all)  >  Main (448)  >  Diamond (198: experts agree,
                                              <=1 of 3 non-experts correct)

Construction pipeline.

  ~1,000 subject experts submit Q (MC or exact short answer, some multimodal)
          |
          v
  LLM difficulty gate: exact-match Qs must stump several frontier LLMs;
  MC Qs must be at or below chance across all LLMs   [10]
          |
          v
  human expert review, two rounds (refine, then approve)
          |
          v
  public set (2,500 Qs) + private held-out set (overfitting detection)
          |
          v
  post-release community bug bounty --> errata; dataset finalised Apr 3, 2025

Architecture.

  hand-built turn-based game (no instructions, no stated goal)
     levels L1..Ln, each with a target state
          |
   agent <-- frame (grid + available_actions + levels_completed) --+
     |                                                             |
     +-- action (ACTION1..ACTION7, reset) --------------------------+
          |
  per-level action count A_ai  vs  human baseline A_h
          |
  RHAE_level = min(cap, (A_h / A_ai)^2);  0 if A_ai > 5 * A_h
          |
  average across levels, games, private environments

Architecture.

  weekly contests (LeetCode / AtCoder / Codeforces)
        |  scrape statement + tests, tag release_date, difficulty
        v
  release_v1 (400 probs, to Mar 2024) ... release_v6 (1055 probs, to Apr 2025)
        |
        v
  scenarios: codegeneration | selfrepair | testoutputprediction | codeexecution
        |
        v
  score only problems with release_date > model cutoff  (--start_date)

Architecture.

  41 repos total
  +-----------------------+   +------------------------+   +-----------------------+
  | PUBLIC (731 tasks)    |   | HELD-OUT (858 tasks)   |   | COMMERCIAL (276)      |
  | 11 copyleft (GPL)     |   | 12 separate repos,     |   | 18 proprietary startup|
  | repos, released on HF |   | kept private to detect |   | repos; results public,|
  |                       |   | future overfitting     |   | data never released   |
  +-----------------------+   +------------------------+   +-----------------------+
                \__________________ human-verified, augmented _______________/
                        (issue text + requirements + interface spec)

7.2 tau-bench, tau2-bench and tau3-bench: tool-agent-user loops graded by end state

  +------------------+   natural language   +-----------------+   API calls
  | user simulator   | <------------------> |  agent under    | -------------> +-------+
  | (LLM + scenario, |                      |  test (policy   |                | DB /  |
  |  optional tools) |                      |  doc + tools)   | <------------- | env   |
  +------------------+                      +-----------------+   results      +-------+
                                                                                   |
   reward = DB_state_hash(final) == DB_state_hash(replay(gold_actions))           |
            x  all communicate_info strings present in agent messages    <---------+

7.5 OSWorld and OSWorld-Verified

  VM snapshot --config scripts--> initial state --> agent loop:
      screenshot / a11y tree --> model --> pyautogui action (max_steps budget)
                                                   |
  evaluator script: read files/app state/URLs from VM --> compare to expected --> 0/1

Architecture.

  task suites with human baselines (HCAST, RE-Bench, SWAA, ...)
     each task: human_minutes (geo-mean of successful human baselines)
          |
  agent runs --> score_binarized in {0,1}, weighted (diversity-adjusted)
          |
  fit logistic:  P(success) = sigma( a - b * log2(human_minutes) )
          |
  horizon_50 = human_minutes where P = 0.5   (also p80)
  bootstrap over tasks/runs --> CI;  regress log(horizon) on release date

Grading.

  request + reference files --> model deliverable  \
                            --> expert deliverable  >--> blinded occupational expert
                                                    /    ranks unlabeled outputs
                                                         (>1 hour per comparison)
  metric: win-or-tie rate vs. human expert
  automated grader (experimental, gold subset): 66% agreement with experts
  vs. 71% human inter-rater agreement

03. Eval Harnesses and Platforms: The Architecture of Fourteen Systems

1. The common skeleton

  +-----------+    +-------------+    +-----------+    +----------+    +-----------+

                         inspect eval / eval_set()

      |

      |

04. LLM-as-a-Judge: Protocols, Biases, Calibration, Ensembles, Fine-Tuned Judges and Judge Alignment

1. The canonical judge loop

             +-------------------+      +------------------------------+
 inputs ---->| System under test |----->| candidate output(s)          |
 (prompt,    +-------------------+      | A (and B for pairwise)       |
  context)                              +--------------+---------------+
                                                       |
   +--------------------+   +----------------------+   |
   | criteria / rubric  |   | reference answer     |   |
   | (from spec, expert,|   | (optional; gold or   |   |
   |  or EvalGen-style  |   |  judge-generated)    |   |
   |  elicitation)      |   +----------+-----------+   |
   +---------+----------+              |               |
             v                         v               v
        +--------------------------------------------------------+
        | Judge prompt assembly: task, criteria, few-shot        |
        | anchors, order randomisation / swap, output schema     |
        +---------------------------+----------------------------+
                                    v
        +--------------------------------------------------------+
        | Judge model(s): CoT -> verdict (score / label / A|B)   |
        | optional: logprobs, n samples, multiple judges         |
        +---------------------------+----------------------------+
                                    v
        +--------------------------------------------------------+
        | Parse + aggregate: swap-consistency, vote/average,     |
        | Bradley-Terry fit, length/style regression, bootstrap  |
        +---------------------------+----------------------------+
                                    v
        +--------------------------------------------------------+
        | Meta-evaluation: agreement with human labels (kappa,   |
        | MCC, F1, failure coverage), separability, CIs, drift   |
        +--------------------------------------------------------+

3.2 G-Eval: form-filling with auto-CoT and probability-weighted scores

 criteria text --> [LLM: generate evaluation steps (auto-CoT)] --> steps
                                                                    |
 (source, output) + steps --> [LLM: fill form, score 1..N] --> token probs
                                                                    |
                         score = sum_i  p(score = i) * i   <--------+

3.3 Pairwise against an anchor: AlpacaEval and Arena-Hard

 Chatbot Arena prompts --> embed + cluster into topics
         |
         v
 LLM annotator scores each prompt on 7 criteria
 (specificity, domain knowledge, complexity, problem-solving,
  creativity, technical accuracy, real-world application)
         |
         v
 keep clusters with mean score >= 6/7 (250 clusters) --> sample 2 each
         |
         v
 500 prompts x (model vs GPT-4-0314 baseline) x 2 orders = 1,000 judgments
         |
         v
 judge (GPT-4-Turbo): CoT, writes own answer first, then 5-way label
 A>>B, A>B, A~=B, B>A, B>>A
         |
         v
 Bradley-Terry fit over all comparisons, 100x bootstrap -> score + 95% CI

3.4 Rubric grading at scale: HealthBench

 conversation + rubric items [(criterion_i, points_i, tags_i)]
          |
          +--> for each item i:  grader(conversation, criterion_i)
          |        -> {"explanation": ..., "criteria_met": true|false}
          v
 score = sum(points_i for met items) / sum(points_i where points_i > 0)
          |
          v
 example scores -> mean clipped to [0,1] -> bootstrap std (1,000 resamples)
          -> per-tag (theme / axis) breakdowns

5.3 Selective judging with guarantees: Trust or Escalate

 item --> [Mistral-7B judge] --conf >= t1--> accept verdict
              | abstain
              v
          [GPT-3.5 judge] ----conf >= t2--> accept verdict
              | abstain
              v
          [GPT-4-Turbo judge] -conf >= t3--> accept verdict
              | abstain
              v
          escalate to human
 thresholds t_i calibrated on a held-out human-labelled split
 (reference config: calibration set 500, alpha = 0.15, delta = 0.1)

8.1 EvalGen: "Who validates the validators?"

 developer prompt + sample (vars, prompt, response) triples
          |
          v
 [LLM] suggest evaluation criteria  --> developer edits / confirms
          |
          v
 per criterion: [LLM AssertionEmitter] generate multiple candidate
 evaluators (code assertions or LLM-judge prompts), streamed
          |
          v
 Executor (background): run every candidate on every example;
 update each example's grading priority from candidates' pass rates
          |
          v
 developer grades examples thumbs up/down, choosing next example by
 "priority" policy (or random) to get a mix of good and bad outputs
          |
          v
 per candidate: confusion matrix vs. developer grades ->
   F1, MCC, Cohen's kappa, failure coverage, false-failure rate
          |
          v
 select best candidate per criterion (sorted by MCC, then lower FFR)
 -> report card: coverage of failures, false failures, chosen set

10. A reference architecture for a production judge service

             +------------------- offline (alignment) --------------------+
             |  expert labels + critiques (binary, principal expert)       |
             |        |                                                    |
             |        v                                                    |
             |  criteria elicitation (EvalGen-style) <--- criteria drift   |
             |        |                                                    |
             |        v                                                    |
             |  candidate judges (prompt variants / models / fine-tunes)   |
             |        |                                                    |
             |        v                                                    |
             |  meta-eval: MCC, kappa, failure coverage, FFR, swap-        |
             |  consistency, adversarial set (LLMBar/JudgeBench-like)      |
             |        |                                                    |
             |        v                                                    |
             |  optimise (few-shot, MemAlign/GEPA) -> freeze + version     |
             +--------+---------------------------------------------------+
                      |
 online / CI          v
 outputs --> per-criterion judges (isolated; rubric items; CoT -> JSON)
               |  pairwise? -> both orders; cross-family panel if comparative
               v
          aggregate: rubric points, vote/mean, Bradley-Terry + style
          covariates, clustered SEs / bootstrap CIs
               |
               +--> low confidence / abstain --> escalate (bigger judge, human)
               v
          dashboards + sampled human audit --> drift alarms --> back to offline

05. Human Evaluation and Arenas: Pairwise Preference Platforms, Their Statistics, Their Critics, and Expert Annotation Pipelines

 user prompt (any language, any task)
        |
        v
+--------------------+    sampling weights     +----------------------------+
|  Battle scheduler  |<------------------------|  Policy: new models w=5    |
|  picks (A, B) pair |                         |  until 3k votes; top-10 w=3|
+---------+----------+                         |  best of top-10 providers  |
          |                                    +----------------------------+
          v
+--------------------+   two anonymous, position-randomized responses
|  Serving layer     |---------------------------------------------------+
|  (FastChat / APIs) |                                                   |
+--------------------+                                                   v
                                                           +---------------------+
                                                           | Vote UI: A / B /    |
                                                           | tie / both bad      |
                                                           +----------+----------+
                                                                      | identities
                                                                      | revealed
                                                                      v  after vote
+--------------------+   +------------------+   +-----------------------------------+
| Abuse defenses     |-->| Battle log       |-->| Offline analytics                 |

| tests, Cloudflare  |   +------------------+   | -> anchored scale -> leaderboard  |
+--------------------+                          +-----------------------------------+

2.2 Bradley-Terry as logistic regression

For battle i, X_i in R^M has +1 at the left model, -1 at the right model.
Y_i = 1 if the left model wins, else 0.

beta_hat = argmin_beta  (1/n) * sum_i BCE( sigmoid(X_i . beta), Y_i )

Reported Arena Score = 400 * beta (natural-log scale converted via log(10))
                       + 1000, then shifted so an anchor model has a fixed value.

 topic clusters)                  (LLM judge, CI,   accuracy / downstream
        |                         separability)     RLHF correlation)

1. The product-eval stack at a glance

                 +---------------------------------------------------------+

                 +-------------------------+-------------------------------+
                                           | sample (random / outlier /
                                           | failure-driven / stratified)
                                           v
  synthetic  --->+---------------------------------------------------------+

  tuples)        |   axial coding -> 5-10 failure categories, counts      |
                 +-------------------------+-------------------------------+
                                           | rank by frequency x impact
                       +-------------------+-------------------+
                       v                                       v
          +--------------------------+           +--------------------------+

          +------------+-------------+           +------------+-------------+
                       |                     +----------------+---------------+
                       |                     v                                v
                       |       +--------------------------+  +----------------------+
                       |       | code assertion (regex,   |  | LLM judge: binary,   |

                       |       +------------+-------------+  | TPR/TNR-validated    |
                       |                    |                +----------+-----------+
                       v                    v                           v

                     | every commit / PR     | scheduled             | release
                     v                       v                       v
              CI gate (cheap,         offline judge runs,       A/B test on

6.1 Cadence and gating

 developer change (prompt / model / retrieval / code)
        |
        v
 +--------------+    PR opened     +-------------------------------------+
 | local run    |----------------->| CI job                              |
 | (cached LLM  |                  |  1. run golden set through system   |
 |  calls)      |                  |  2. deterministic asserts (all)     |
 +--------------+                  |  3. validated judges (subset/cache) |
                                   |  4. compare vs baseline experiment  |
                                   |  5. gate: --fail-on-error, or       |
                                   |     pass-rate >= threshold, or      |
                                   |     no regression on key scores     |
                                   +---------+----------------+----------+
                                             |                |
                              PR comment w/ score diffs   JUnit/JSON/HTML
                              (+3pp, 8 up / 4 down)       artifacts, dashboards

7.1 EvalGen and criteria drift

  user context + prompt
          |
          v
  [1] LLM proposes criteria (NL) <---- user edits/adds/removes, picks
          |                             code-based or LLM-based per criterion
          v
  [2] LLM generates candidate assertions per criterion (Python fns or
      judge prompts), executed on sampled outputs in background
          |
          v
  [3] user grades outputs thumbs-up / thumbs-down (no fixed quota)
          |     a thumbs-up output must pass all criteria; candidates that
          |     fail good outputs or pass bad ones are re-weighted
          v
  [4] select per-criterion assertion best aligned with grades
          |
          v
  report card: per-criterion confusion matrix, coverage, FFR

7.3 Statistical validation: TPR, TNR, splits and bias correction

 expert labels (~100, balanced)
     |
     +--> train 10-20% --> few-shot pool --+
     |                                     v
     +--> dev 40-45% ---> run judge --> TPR/TNR --> inspect FP/FN --> edit prompt
     |                                     ^                              |
     |                                     +------------------------------+
     +--> test 40-45% --> run ONCE --> final TPR, TNR (+ bootstrap CI)
                                              |
 production traces --> judge --> p_obs --> Rogan-Gladen --> corrected rate +/- CI

                         SYNCHRONOUS (request path, latency-bound)

              |   block/steer            |  spans + events (OTel GenAI)       |  block/
              v                          v                                    v  edit
 +-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 |  telemetry pipeline: SDK -> OTel Collector (redact PII, sample, route) -> backend    |
 +-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
                         ASYNCHRONOUS (minutes to hours, cost-bound)
     |                  |                      |                       |
     v                  v                      v                       v
 [rules: filter   [online evaluators:    [feedback join:         [experiment platform:
  + sample %]      code checks, LLM       thumbs, edits,          assignment, OEC,
     |             judges, classifiers]   acceptance, retention]  guardrail metrics]
     |                  |                      |                       |
     +---------> scores attached to spans (gen_ai.evaluation.result) <-+

   alerts]         humans label,        traces become         clusters, score trends,
