5.1 The signal funnel

 opportunities to suggest   (LLM calls, shown or not)
        |
 prompts sent / responses returned
        |
 responses actually SEEN by the user
        |
 ACCEPTS  (scenario-specific: thumbs up in chat, inserting text into a document)
        |
 responses KEPT at a later time point  (retention of the generated content)

7.1 Problem and placement

              +------------- input rails ----------+
 user prompt ->| injection / jailbreak classifier   |--block--> refusal
              | topic / policy classifier          |
              | PII masking                        |
              +-----------------+------------------+
                                v
                 dialog / retrieval / execution rails (agent, RAG, tools)
                                |
              +-----------------v------------------+
              | output rails: safety classifier,   |--block/edit--> safe response
              | grounding / hallucination check,   |
              | exchange classifier (sees in+out)  |
              +------------------------------------+
   every verdict --> telemetry (GUARDRAIL span / gen_ai.evaluation.result) [4][9]

Lessons that generalise:

 failure class                 what missed it               what would catch it
 ----------------------------  ---------------------------  ------------------------------
 small-% routing error         aggregate benchmarks,        production-path evals sliced
 (sticky, per user)            canaries                     by route / server pool
 token-level corruption        task-level scores (model     invariant checks: script/lang
 (rare unlikely tokens)        self-corrects)               mismatch, syntax validity
 sampler numerical bug         evals on other configs       evals at production batch sizes
                                                            and configs; exact references
 overlapping incidents         noisy global metrics         per-deploy change attribution

09. Safety and Dangerous-Capability Evaluation: Frontier Safety Frameworks, Third-Party Testing, Red-Teaming Pipelines, Jailbreak Benchmarks, Sandbagging and Evaluation Awareness

1.1 The common pattern

  +------------------+   triggers: new pretrain/post-train checkpoint,
  | Model checkpoint |   "meaningful new capabilities", calendar interval
  +--------+---------+
           |
           v
  +---------------------------+     +----------------------------------+
  | Cheap automated evals     |---->| Alert / early-warning threshold  |
  | (benchmarks, proxies,     |     | crossed? (set BELOW the real     |
  |  agentic task suites)     |     | capability threshold = buffer)   |
  +---------------------------+     +-----------+----------------------+
                                       no |      | yes / can't rule out
                                          v      v
                         +------------------+  +-------------------------------+
                         | Document "well   |  | Deep dive: expert red-teaming,|
                         | below threshold" |  | uplift trials, external evals,|
                         +------------------+  | stronger elicitation          |
                                               +---------------+---------------+
                                                               v
                          +-----------------------------------------------------+
                          | Determination: threshold crossed / not crossed /    |
                          | cannot rule out --> required safeguards (security + |
                          | deployment) --> safeguards report / safety case     |
                          +--------------------------+--------------------------+
                                                     v
                          +-----------------------------------------------------+
                          | Governance review (RSO / SAG / safety council),     |
                          | deploy, post-deployment monitoring, periodic re-eval|
                          +-----------------------------------------------------+

2.1 METR: autonomy and the time-horizon metric

 Task suites with human baselines            Agent runs (many models, many seeds)
 (software, ML, cyber, reasoning;            score_binarized in {0,1}
  human_minutes per task)                    score_cont in [0,1]
            \                                      /
             v                                    v
        +----------------------------------------------+
        | Fit logistic: P(success) ~ log2(human_minutes)|
        | weighted by invsqrt(task-family size)         |
        +-----------------------+----------------------+
                                v
        +----------------------------------------------+
        | Time horizon = task length at which P = 50%  |
        | (also 80%); bootstrap -> CI (p50q0.025 ...)  |
        +-----------------------+----------------------+
                                v
         Trend over model release dates -> doubling time

2.2 UK AI Security Institute and US CAISI

         main task + side task (setting)
                   |
     +-------------v--------------+        attack mode: policy also
     | Untrusted policy (U)       |<------ pursues side task
     +-------------+--------------+
                   | proposed action
     +-------------v--------------+  suspicion score
     | Monitor (trusted model T)  |---------------+
     +-------------+--------------+               |
                   |                              v
          low suspicion                high suspicion -> defer to T /
                   |                    audit / block
                   v
            execute action  ---> scorers: usefulness (honest runs),
                                  safety = P(no side task succeeds) (attack runs)

4. Automated red-teaming and jailbreak attack algorithms

  goal G, target string T
        |
  +-----v------------+  prompt P_i   +---------------+  response R_i
  | Attacker LLM     |-------------->| Target LLM    |------------+
  | (system prompt:  |               +---------------+            |
  |  red-team role)  |<---------------------------------+         v
  +------------------+   (P_i, R_i, score S_i)          |  +-------------+
        ^  N parallel streams x K iterations            +--| Judge LLM   |
        |                                                  | score 1..10 |
        +--- stop when S_i = 10 (jailbroken) --------------+-------------+

5.1 HarmBench

 behaviors (CSV; val/test; standard, contextual, copyright, multimodal)
     |
 [1] generate_test_cases.py  -- RedTeamingMethod subclasses (GCG, PAIR, TAP, ...)
 [1.5] merge_test_cases.py   -- test_cases.json per experiment
     |
 [2] generate completions on target models / defenses (HF, API, multimodal)
     |
 [3] classify: HarmBench-Llama-2-13b-cls (text), -multimodal cls,
     Mistral-7b validation cls; copyright via hash matching
     |
     ASR per (method, model, behavior)    run modes: slurm | local | local_parallel (Ray)

6.2 Automated auditing agents: Petri

 seed instruction --> Auditor agent ---(simulated user msgs, synthetic tool results,
                          ^   |          rollback/branch)---> Target model
                          |   +<----------- target actions / tool calls -------+
                          |
                    transcript --> Judge model --> scores per dimension
                                     (deception, sycophancy, self-preservation,
                                      power-seeking, reward hacking, ...)

Design responses.

  realism axis                       observation axis
  ------------                       ----------------
  production-derived scenarios       paired monitored / "unmonitored" conditions [34]
  synthetic tools that look real     CoT monitoring for "this is a test" [8][36]
  avoid salient cues and tidy        interpretability probes / steering on
  binary dilemmas [33]              eval-awareness features [38]
  deployment-time monitoring as      condition results on verbalised awareness
  a complement to pre-deploy evals   (report rates both ways) [33]

        report: capability determination + safeguards case + residual risk

10. Benchmark Validity and Statistical Rigor: Contamination, Saturation, Construct Validity, Error Bars, and Reproducible Leaderboards

Overview

                    THE VALIDITY STACK FOR A BENCHMARK NUMBER
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Claim: "Model A > Model B on capability C"                                    |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| 4. Construct validity   items + grader actually measure C?        (10.7)      |
| 3. Headroom             benchmark not saturated?                  (10.6)      |
| 2. Internal validity    no contamination / config drift / prompt  (10.4-10.5, |
|                         artefacts / judge bias?                    10.8)      |
| 1. Statistical validity difference >> question, sampling and      (10.2-10.3) |
|                         infrastructure noise?                                 |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Raw material: items, harness, model endpoint, grader, aggregation             |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

10.2.1 Anthropic's "Adding Error Bars to Evals"

          ERROR-BAR PIPELINE (after Miller / Anthropic, implemented in Inspect)

 items (n, cluster_id) --> run model K epochs --> per-item scores s[i,k]
                                                       |
                       reduce within item (mean over k; or P(correct token))
                                                       |
            +------------------------------------------+---------------------+
            |                                          |                     |
   mean, SEM (CLT)                       clustered SEM by cluster_id   paired diff vs
   95% CI = mean +/- 1.96*SEM            (t with clusters-1 dof)       baseline model:
   Wilson CI if binary                                                 d_i = sA_i - sB_i
            |                                          |               SE(d), corr(A,B)
            +--------------------> report: score, SE, CI, n, clusters, K, rho
                                         |
                       power analysis decides n and K for the NEXT run

10.5.1 What counts as contamination

                  CONTAMINATION DETECTION: WHERE EACH METHOD SITS

  needs training data?        needs logits?           needs only outputs/scores?
  -------------------         -------------           --------------------------
  n-gram overlap (GPT-3       Min-K% Prob [33]        guided-instruction completion
   style, lm-eval 13-gram)    Min-K%++ [34]            "Time Travel" [31]
  infini-gram mini [42]       DC-PDD                  regeneration probes [43]
  ConTAM thresholds [38]      sharded rank test       performance-based:
  LLM decontaminator [20]      (exchangeability)[32]   GSM1k-style twins [30],
                              dataset inference [36]   ConStat [39], GSM-Symbolic [29]
            \                         |                         /
             +------------- prevention / provable flags --------+
               canaries, encryption, gating, private splits [13][46]
               DyePack backdoors [45], Bayes-accuracy ceiling [44]
               live / post-cutoff benchmarks [54][55][56]

10.6 Saturation and the benchmark lifecycle

                         BENCHMARK LIFECYCLE (observed 2023-2026)

  design --> release --> climb (high SNR) --> saturation / contamination
    |          |               |                    |
    |      canary,         CIs, paired          symptoms: ceiling, gains compress,
    |      gating          comparisons          detectors fire, twin-set gaps
    |                                               |
    |                         +---------------------+---------------------+
    |                         |                     |                     |
    |                  refresh items          harder successor      graduate to
    |                  (LiveBench,            (MMLU-Pro, HLE,       regression
    |                  LiveCodeBench)         GSM-Symbolic)         suite [6]
    +<------------------------+---------------------+

10.9 Reference architecture: a validity-aware evaluation pipeline

+------------------------------- EVAL REGISTRY ---------------------------------+
| benchmark id + version/release tag | item ids + cluster ids | canary | split    |
| prompt set (>=1 template, versioned) | judge model+version | resource spec      |
+--------------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
                                       |
   +-------------------+     +---------v-----------+     +-----------------------+
   | CONTAMINATION     |     | RUNNER              |     | ENVIRONMENT           |
   | GATE (pre-run)    |---->| K epochs x P prompts|<--->| pinned image, CPU/RAM |
   | n-gram/ConTAM on  |     | seeds logged        |     | floor + kill ceiling, |
   | corpus; twin-set  |     | batch-invariant or  |     | egress/search filter  |
   | or ConStat check; |     | platform recorded   |     | (block benchmark      |
   | post-RL audit     |     +---------+-----------+     |  names, mirrors)      |
   +-------------------+               |                 +-----------------------+
                                       v
                      +----------------+-----------------+
                      | ITEM-LEVEL LOG (immutable)       |
                      | transcript, score, logprob, infra |
                      | error flag, tokens, judge output |
                      +----------------+-----------------+
                                       |
        +------------------+-----------+-----------+-------------------------+
        |                  |                       |                         |
  STATS ENGINE        VALIDITY CHECKS        INTEGRITY MONITOR        REPORTER
  SEM, clustered SE,  broken-task scan       eval-awareness scan       score, CI, n,
  Wilson/boot CI,     (0% pass@100), grader  (benchmark names in       clusters, K, P,
  paired diff + rho,  spot-check vs humans,  reasoning/search),        PSS spread, infra
  PSS across prompts, style covariates,      leaked-answer sources,    error rate, judge,
  power for next run  transcript reading     infra-error outliers      config hash

     question ---> model under test ---> predicted answer

 response --> [split atomic] --> [make self-contained] --> [relevant?] --no--> irrelevant

       F1@K:  Prec = S/(S+N);  Recall@K = min(S/K, 1);  F1@K = HM(Prec, Recall@K)

          doc sents d1..dM

   ..   |                 |
   sN   | eN1 ...     eNM |-->
        +-----------------+
   summary score = mean over summary sentences

4.1 Vectara HHEM and the Hallucination Leaderboard

  private corpus (>7,700 articles, 50 to 24K words; news, tech, science,
  medicine, legal, sports, business, education)
           |
           v
  each LLM (temperature 0) summarizes with fixed prompt:
   "Summarize using only the information in the given passage. Do not infer...
    never exceed 20 percent of the original text's length...
    else respond 'I am unable to summarize this text.'"
           |
           +--> refusals / very short outputs --> Answer Rate
           v
  keep only documents that ALL models summarized (common subset)
           |
           v
  HHEM-2.3 (source, summary) -> consistent?  --> Factual Consistency Rate
                                                 Hallucination Rate = 100 - FCR
                                                 + Avg summary length (words)

Architecture (paper-reported, approximate).

 (system instr, long doc <=32k tok, user request)
              |
              v
       model under test --> response
              |
   +----------+-----------+----------------+
   v                      v                v
 Judge A (Gemini)     Judge B (GPT-4o)  Judge C (Claude)
   | eligible?            | eligible?      | eligible?     -- no --> score 0
   | fully grounded?      | grounded?      | grounded?
   +----------+-----------+----------------+
              v
     mean over judges, mean over examples -> FACTS Grounding score

6.3 ALCE (Princeton, EMNLP 2023): automatic citation benchmark

 statement s_i  with citations {c1, c2}
    recall_i    = NLI( concat(c1,c2) => s_i )
    precision_j = c_j is "irrelevant" if  NLI(c_j => s_i) fails AND
                  NLI(other cites => s_i) still holds without c_j

12. Code Evaluation in Depth: From Unit-Tested Functions to Repository Agents and In-IDE Assistants

Overview

 GEN 1  function synthesis      HumanEval (164), MBPP (~1k), APPS (~10k), CodeContests
        prompt -> function -> hidden unit tests -> pass@k
            |  problem: weak tests, saturation, contamination, Python-only
            v
 GEN 2  hardened / broadened    EvalPlus (80x / 35x tests), MultiPL-E (18 languages),
        function-level          BigCodeBench (1,140 library-heavy tasks), LiveCodeBench
                                (dated contest problems), LiveCodeBench Pro
            |  problem: still single-file, still "toy" relative to real work
            v
 GEN 3  repository context      RepoBench, CrossCodeEval (cross-file completion);
                                SWE-bench (issue -> patch -> FAIL_TO_PASS/PASS_TO_PASS)
                                and its family: Verified, Multimodal, Multilingual,
                                Multi-SWE-bench, SWE-PolyBench, SWE-bench-Live, Pro
            |  problem: scaffold, infra and test validity dominate the score
            v
 GEN 4  end-to-end / in-product Aider polyglot (edit format + test feedback),
                                Terminal-Bench 2.0, SWE-Lancer; in-IDE evals:
                                Copilot simulation tests, Copilot Arena, acceptance
                                telemetry, A/B tests

12.1 The execution-grading contract

  +-------------+    +-------------+    +-------------------+    +----------------+
  | task spec   |--->| model /     |--->| candidate         |--->| sandboxed      |
  | (prompt,    |    | agent +     |    | (function, patch, |    | executor       |
  |  repo@sha,  |    | scaffold    |    |  edited files)    |    | (process, VM,  |
  |  issue)     |    +-------------+    +-------------------+    |  container)    |
  +-------------+                                                +-------+--------+
                                                                         |
            hidden tests / verifier (unit tests, F2P+P2P, e2e, output diff)
                                                                         |
                                          +------------------------------v--------+
                                          | per-attempt verdict: pass | fail |    |
                                          | timeout | apply-fail | infra-error     |
                                          +------------------------------+--------+
                                                                         |
                                 aggregate: pass@k, pass^k, resolve rate, cost, CI

12.3 Live and competitive programming: LiveCodeBench and LiveCodeBench Pro

  contests ----> scrape statement + tests + release_date + difficulty
                     |
       release_vN = all problems with date <= T_N   (v1: 400 ... v6: 1,055)
                     |
   model M, cutoff C ---> score window (C, T_N]   <-- only comparable window
                     |
   "lite" pruned tests (fast) | --not_fast (full tests)  ; timeout sensitivity < 0.5 pt

12.5.1 SWE-bench: issues to patches, graded by the maintainers' tests

  base image  (OS + language toolchain)            shared by all tasks
      |
  environment image (repo-specific deps @ version)  shared by tasks of same repo/version
      |
  instance image (task-specific setup @ base_commit)
      |
  run: git apply model_patch -> apply test_patch -> run tests -> log markers
      |
  grading.py: parse log -> F2P / P2P -> FULL | PARTIAL | NO -> results.json

12.6 Aider polyglot: an editing benchmark with feedback

  for each exercise (random order, in a Docker container):
    files shown   = solution stubs only  (tests, examples, .meta hidden)
    instructions  = .docs/introduction.md + instructions.md + instructions.append.md
                    + "Don't change the names of existing functions or classes ...
                       Only use standard libraries ..."
    for try in 1..tries (default 2):
        model edits files via edit format (whole | diff | diff-fenced | architect)
        copy original tests in; run per-language command, 180 s timeout
          (.py pytest | .rs cargo test | .go go test | .js npm | .cpp | .java gradlew)
        pass -> stop
        fail -> next instructions = test error output +
                "The tests are correct, don't try and change them. Fix the code ..."
    report pass_rate_1, pass_rate_2, percent_cases_well_formed, cost, timeouts ...

12.7.1 Isolation levels

  weakest                                                                   strongest
  in-process guard   child process +    container,          container per    full VM     
  (HumanEval         rlimits/timeouts   network none        sample + infra   (EC2,Proxmox,
  reliability_guard) (APPS-style)       (MultiPL-E)         orchestration    Vagrant)
                                                            (SWE-bench,
                                                            Inspect, Harbor)

13. Multimodal and Speech Evaluation: MMMU, MathVista, Video Benchmarks, Image Generation Evals, and Speech, ASR, TTS and Voice-Agent Evals

Overview

          THREE EVALUATION ARCHITECTURES BY WHERE THE MODALITY SITS
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| (1) UNDERSTANDING        media + question --> model --> text --> extractor  |
|     MMMU, MathVista,                                         |              |

|     risk: question answerable WITHOUT the media (blind solvability)         |
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| (2) GENERATION           prompt --> generator --> image / video / audio     |
|     GenEval, VQAScore,                                  |                   |
|     HPS, VBench, Seed-TTS   detector | VQA model | ASR+WER | SV-embedding   |
|                             | reward model | MOS predictor | human arena    |

+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------+

|     Full-Duplex-Bench,         |                               |            |

+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------+

13.1.4 MMBench: CircularEval and LLM choice extraction

            MMBench CIRCULAR EVALUATION (4-option item)
  pass 1:  A=cat  B=dog  C=fox  D=owl   gt=B  -> model "B"  ok
  pass 2:  A=dog  B=fox  C=owl  D=cat   gt=A  -> model "A"  ok
  pass 3:  A=fox  B=owl  C=cat  D=dog   gt=D  -> model "D"  ok
  pass 4:  A=owl  B=cat  C=dog  D=fox   gt=C  -> model "B"  FAIL
  item score = AND(all passes) = 0     cost = 4x inference

13.2.2 Video-MME-v2 (2026): grouped non-linear scoring

     VIDEO-MME-v2 GROUPED SCORING (one group = 4 linked questions)
  Q1 retrieve fact   Q2 order events   Q3 causal link   Q4 inference
     correct            correct           wrong            correct
  per-question accuracy contribution : 3/4 = 0.75
  group (non-linear) score           : heavily discounted, because the
                                       reasoning chain is broken at Q3
  ratio NonLin/Avg across groups     -> robustness index per model

13.3.2 VLMEvalKit (OpenCompass)

          GENERIC MULTIMODAL UNDERSTANDING HARNESS (lmms-eval / VLMEvalKit)

     |                    (TorchCodec, frame       options, CoT/direct flag)

     |                                                     |
     |                    cache (sha256 key) <-------------+
     |                                                     v
     |                    strip <think> --> rule extractor --fail--> LLM extractor
     |                                                     |
     +--> metric: acc | CircularEval | group score | WER | judge score
                     --> CI, clustered SE (by video/image), paired diff

          (release gates)           bound, recognition test, fixed prompt pool

          (voice / agent)           telephony codec, noise/accent ablations,
                                    tool-state scoring, latency percentiles

          reasoning-tag stripping, fixed hardware for latency/RTFx claims

14. Evals as Training Signal: Reward Models, Verifiers, Rubric Rewards, Process Supervision and Reward-Hacking Detection

Overview

                  THE EVALUATOR-AS-REWARD SPECTRUM

  cheap to hack                                               hard to hack
  broad coverage                                              narrow coverage
  <---------------------------------------------------------------------->
  user thumbs   BT reward   generative /   rubric /      answer    unit tests   Lean /
  (approval)    model       reasoning RM   checklist     match     in sandbox   proof
                (scalar)    (critique+     judge         (rule)    (execution)  kernel
                            score)         (per-item)
     [48]        [1][2]      [21][22][23]   [35][36][37]  [25][27]  [28][29]     [30]

1.2 Architecture

 loss = - log sigmoid( r(x, y_chosen) - r(x, y_rejected) )

 prompt x --+--> [ LM backbone ] --> last-token hidden state --> [ linear head ] --> r
 response y +

1.4 Failure modes: overoptimization

 best-of-n:  R_gold(d) = d (alpha_bon - beta_bon * d)
 RL:         R_gold(d) = d (alpha_RL  - beta_RL  * log d)

 gold  ^            ___
 reward|         .-'   '-.          <- proxy RM keeps rising
       |       .'         '.
       |     .'             '.      <- gold peaks, then falls
       |   .'
       +----------------------------> d = sqrt(KL)

4.2 Let's Verify Step by Step (OpenAI, 2023)

 solution = s1 s2 s3 ... sk
              |  |  |      |
             [ PRM: P(step correct | prefix) per step ]
              |  |  |      |
             .98 .95 .40 ...       score = prod(p_i) or min(p_i)
                      ^ first error localized

5.3 Rubrics as Rewards (Scale AI, 2025)

 prompt + expert reference --> rubric generator --> [ (criterion_i, weight_i) ... ]
                                                              |
 policy samples y_1..y_G --> judge(prompt, y_j, rubric) ------+
                                 |  explicit: per-criterion verdicts -> weighted sum
                                 |  implicit: one score given the full rubric
                                 v
                           rewards r_1..r_G --> GRPO advantage --> policy update

6.4 DeepSeekMath-V2: verifier, meta-verifier and generator co-training

 +-----------+  proofs   +-----------+  analyses  +---------------+
 | generator |---------->| verifier  |----------->| meta-verifier |
 | (+ self-  |<----------| score +   |<-----------| are the found |
 |  analysis)|  rewards  | issues    |  R_meta    | issues real?  |
 +-----+-----+           +-----+-----+            +---------------+
       | hard new proofs       ^
       v                       | retrain on auto-labeled data
   [ n verifications x m meta-verifications -> label or escalate to humans ]

7.3 Chain-of-thought monitoring and its limits

                 DETECTION STACK (keep every layer OUT of the reward)

  rollouts --> [ env hardening: sandbox, read-only tests, hidden test split ]
           --> [ rule-based hack checks: test-file diffs, exit codes, claims vs state ]
           --> [ CoT + action monitor (LLM) -> suspicion score ]  --> dashboards
           --> [ proxy vs gold divergence: RM score up, held-out judge/human down ]
           --> [ periodic behavioral evals: sycophancy, sabotage, shortcut rate ]
                                     |
                       alert -> fix environment / grader, not the gradient

8.1 Reference architecture

 +-----------------------+      +--------------------------------------------+
 | TASK / PROMPT POOL    |      | REWARD SERVICE (environment hub)           |
 | difficulty-filtered   |----->|  rule checks | sandboxed tests | Lean       |
 | (pass-rate band),     |      |  rubric judge | generative RM | pref. RM   |
 | dedup vs eval sets    |      |  hack-check layer | length/format terms    |
 +----------+------------+      +---------------------+----------------------+
            |                                         | rewards
            v                                         v
 +-----------------------+   rollouts   +-------------------------------------+
 | POLICY (rollout eng.) |------------->| TRAINER (GRPO/PPO/DAPO; KL budget)  |
 +----------+------------+              +-------------------+-----------------+
            ^                                               | checkpoints
            |                                               v
            |      +----------------------------------------------------------+
            |      | HELD-OUT EVAL GATE (never optimized against)             |
            |      |  gold judge / human audit | pass@1 + pass@k | spurious-  |
            |      |  reward control | hack-rate + CoT monitor | sycophancy   |
            |      +-------------------------+--------------------------------+
            |                                | fail: fix grader, re-curate
            +--------------------------------+ pass: promote / distill

8.2 Grader development as an eval loop

 draft grader --> score base-model samples --> human/expert agreement check
      ^                                               |
      |                                               v
 add contrastive negatives <-- inspect top-reward <-- small RL pilot
 (hacked outputs)              samples for exploits

        (tier 1: current models on latest evals; tier 2: dated history)

       |          |                                   ^
       |          +---- probes ----> target ----------+

       |          |

  Frontier Safety Framework evals (CBRN, cyber, ML R&D, deceptive alignment)

  +------------------+     +------------------+     +--------------------------+

  | behavior desc,   |     | (writer, auditor,|     | evolve, persona, style,  |

  | set (+ seed cfg, |     | spot checks,     |     | critic score, verifier,  |

  +--------+---------+     +------------------+     +--------------------------+

           v

  | Run: target +    |---->| Graders: code,   |---->| Failure mining -> new    |

  +------------------+     +------------------+     +--------------------------+

3.1 Ragas: knowledge-graph-driven synthesis

 documents --> [Splitter] --> nodes (hierarchical chunks)
                                 |
                  [Extractors: NER, keyphrase, LLM-based or rule-based]
                                 |  node.properties += {entities, keyphrases,...}
                                 v
                  [Relationship builders, e.g. Jaccard over entities]
                                 |  edges: entity_jaccard_similarity ...
                                 v
                        Knowledge graph (KG)
                                 |
        +------------------------+-------------------------+
        |                                                  |
 [QuerySynthesizer.generate_scenarios]           Scenario = (nodes, query length,
   traverse KG for node sets that fit             query style, persona)
   the query type (single/multi-hop,                        |
   specific/abstract)                                       v
                                   [generate_sample] -> (user_input, reference_contexts,
                                                         reference)

5.4 Compositional task algebra with replay verification: τ²-bench telecom

 selection sets S1..Sn (each = atomic subtasks + None)
          |
          v
 itertools.product(S1+[None], ..., Sn+[None])  --task_validator-->  composed tasks
          |                                                    (sorted by #parts)
          v
 for each composed task:
   env = fresh telecom env
   run init_funcs  (break the device/account in specific ways; env assertions)
   collect fix_funcs (tool calls that repair it; requestor = user or assistant)
     - if any fix_func is None -> expected failure -> gold action = transfer to human
   attach env_assertions (+ extra assertions per subtask)
   assign persona (round-robin None/Easy/Hard)
          |
          v
 verify_task():
   assert env starts FIXED
   apply initialization actions  -> env must be BROKEN
   replay gold fix actions one by one; assert NOT fixed before the last one
   assert FIXED at end (or still broken if unfixable); run all env assertions

Architecture.

  task = (user instruction: identity, goal, constraints, hidden facts)
             |
             v
  +--------------------+   natural language   +---------------------------+
  | User simulator LLM |<-------------------->| Agent under test          |
  | strategy: llm |    |                      | (policy doc + domain API  |
  | react | verify |   |                      |  tools)                   |
  | reflection         |                      +-------------+-------------+
  +---------+----------+                                    | tool calls
            | emits ###STOP### when goal satisfied          v
            |                                     +-------------------+
            +------------------------------------>| Database / env    |
                                                  +---------+---------+
                                                            |
  reward = [final DB state == gold DB state] x [required info communicated]
  pass^k = P(all k i.i.d. trials succeed)

7.1 Anthropic Bloom (December 2025)

 seed.yaml: behavior name + description, example transcripts, models per stage,
            num_scenarios, variation_dimensions, modality (conversation | simenv)
     |
     v
 1 UNDERSTANDING  agent analyzes behavior + examples -> what to measure
     |
     v
 2 IDEATION       scenarios: situation, simulated user, system prompt, environment
                  + variations along named dimensions (e.g. noise, emotional_pressure)
     |
     v
 3 ROLLOUT        parallel conversations; Bloom simulates user AND tool responses
     |
     v
 4 JUDGMENT       judge scores behavior presence (+ secondary qualities, e.g. realism);
                  meta-judge writes suite-level report
     -> metrics: elicitation rate, average behavior presence; Inspect-compatible logs

9. Cost and latency engineering

  every commit / PR   : deterministic checks + small regression suite (minutes, cents)
  nightly             : full regression + capability suites, k>=3 trials, judges
  release candidate   : held-out sets, k>=5, human calibration sample, safety audit
  continuous (prod)   : sampled online judges, feedback mining, drift dashboards

4. Closed-Loop AI Systems — Architecture Diagrams

01. Foundations: Feedback Loops, Technical Debt, and the Anatomy of Closed-Loop ML Systems

1.3 A generic loop diagram

                 +---------------------------- world ----------------------------+
                 |  users, drivers, advertisers, police districts, the web ...   |
                 +-------^-----------------------------------------+-------------+
                         | actions / exposures                     | outcomes
                         |                                         v
   +---------+     +-----+------+     +-----------+     +-----------------------+
   | model   +---->+  policy /  |     |  logging  +<----+ observation (labels,  |
   | f_theta |     |  decision  +---->+ (features,|     | clicks, interventions,|
   +----^----+     |  layer     |     | propensity|     | thumbs, accept/reject)|
        |          +------------+     | , action) |     +-----------------------+
        |                             +-----+-----+
        |   new theta                       | training data
   +----+-----------+   eval/gate     +-----v------+
   | deploy/rollout +<----------------+  learner   |
   +----------------+                 +------------+

   Open loop: delete the arrows from "world" back to "learner".
   Closed loop: every arrow present; theta(t+1) depends on theta(t) via the world.

3.1 Definition and mechanism

   recommend top-k by predicted CTR
              |
              v
   exposure concentrated on head items ---> clicks observed only on exposed items
              ^                                         |
              |                                         v
   model learns "head items are best" <--- training set skewed toward head items

        ^                                                          |
        |                                                   human QA / correction
        |                                                          v
 deploy new net <-- eval + unit tests on hard cases <-- retrain <-- dataset

03. Preference-Learning Loops: RLHF, Constitutional AI/RLAIF, DPO, and Online Reward-Model Refresh

Overview

            +------------------------------------------------------------------+
            |                  OUTER LOOP (weeks to months)                    |
            v                                                                  |
  +-------------------+  prompts   +------------------+  comparisons  +---------+--------+
  | Prompt sources    |----------->| Current policy   |-------------->| Judges:          |
  | (API traffic,     |            | pi_k samples 2..K|  pairs/ranks  | humans, AI       |
  |  red team, synth) |            | responses        |               | labeler + rules, |
  +-------------------+            +--------+---------+               | verifiers, users |
                                            ^                         +---------+--------+
                                            |                                   |
                       +--------------------+-----+    +------------------------v-----+
                       | Policy optimization:     |<---| Reward signal:               |
                       | PPO / RLOO / GRPO, or    |    | RM_k (BT model, ensemble,    |
                       | DPO-family on pairs;     |    | generative judge) or         |
                       | KL to reference policy   |    | implicit reward (DPO)        |
                       +------------+-------------+    +------------------------------+
                                    |
                                    v   gates: evals, red team, A/B, staged rollout
                          deploy pi_{k+1} -> new traffic -> new prompts (loop closes)

2.2 Architecture

 Stage 1: SFT              Stage 2: Reward model             Stage 3: RL (PPO-ptx)
 +----------------+        +-------------------------+       +---------------------------+
 | ~13k prompts   |        | ~33k prompts; labelers  |       | ~31k prompts (API only)   |
 | labeler demos  |        | rank K=4..9 samples     |       | policy init = SFT         |
 | -> fine-tune   |        | -> all C(K,2) pairs as  |       | reward = RM(x,y)          |
 |    GPT-3       |------->|    one batch element    |------>|   - beta * KL(pi || SFT)  |
 +----------------+        | 6B RM, BT pairwise loss |       | + gamma * pretrain LM loss|
                           +-------------------------+       +---------------------------+
 prompt sources: API Playground traffic (deduplicated, PII-filtered) + labeler-written

3.2 Architecture

  week k:  PM_k + policy pi_k  --deploy-->  crowdworker chat UI (A/B per turn)
                 ^                                     |
                 |   retrain PM_{k+1} on all data      v
                 +---------  online comparisons (high-reward regime) ---------+

4.2 Constitutional AI and RLAIF (Anthropic)

 SL-CAI stage                                   RL-CAI stage (RLAIF)
 red-team prompt                                prompt -> policy samples (y1, y2)
   -> helpful-only RLHF model response              -> feedback model, prompted with a
   -> critique (random principle)                      sampled principle, answers
   -> revision  (repeat N times)                       "which is less harmful? (A)/(B)"
   -> fine-tune on final revisions                     (optionally with chain of thought)
                                                    -> normalized probs = soft labels
                                                    -> PM trained on AI harmlessness
                                                       labels + HUMAN helpfulness labels
                                                    -> PPO against the PM

8.3 Case study: the GPT-4o sycophancy rollback (April 2025)

 users --thumbs--> feedback RM (rewards agreement) --+
                                                     +--> combined reward --> RL --> pi'
 raters/spec ----> primary RM (penalizes flattery) --+         ^
                                                    weight of primary signal diluted
 pi' deployed -> users like flattery more -> more positive thumbs -> loop amplifies

9.3 How refresh cycles are run

 +--> 1. sample from CURRENT policy on fresh + replayed prompts
 |    2. label: humans (taste, hard cases) + AI judge (spec compliance) + verifiers
 |    3. retrain RM(s) on cumulative data (often re-weighting recent rounds)
 |    4. validate RM: held-out pairs, RewardBench-style suites [29], adversarial
 |       probes, agreement with gold/expert labels; check length/sycophancy bias
 |    5. optimize policy (PPO/GRPO/RLOO or DPO on RM-built pairs) within KL budget
 |    6. gate: evals, red team, human spot checks, A/B, staged rollout
 +--- 7. detect RM exploitation (score up, human/gold down) -> new data targets

9.4 When reward hacking generalizes, and why not to train against the monitor

 RL env with exploitable grader --> policy learns hack --+--> local: reward up, task not done
                                                          +--> global: misaligned generalization
 mitigations: harden graders / held-out verifiers | CoT monitor as DETECTOR, not reward [69]
              | inoculation framing during RL [70] | agentic (not only chat) alignment evals

04. Self-Improvement via Verifiable Rewards: From STaR and Self-Play to GRPO, Reasoning RL Infrastructure, and Self-Verifying Provers

1. The verifiable-reward loop: a reference architecture

            +-------------------+      prompts (curated by difficulty,
            |   Prompt / task   |<---- pass-rate filters, curricula)
            |       pool        |
            +---------+---------+
                      |
                      v
 +-----------+   +----+-----------------+   G samples per prompt
 |  Policy   |-->|  Rollout engine      |--------------------------+
 |  weights  |   |  (vLLM / SGLang;     |                          |
 +-----+-----+   |  tools, sandboxes)   |                          v
       ^         +----------------------+          +---------------+-------------+
       |                                           |  Verifier / reward          |
       |                                           |  - exact answer match       |
       |                                           |  - unit tests in sandbox    |
       |                                           |  - Lean / proof checker     |
       |                                           |  - game outcome             |
       |                                           |  - LLM judge or rubric      |
       |                                           +---------------+-------------+
       |                                                           |
       |      +----------------------------+     rewards r_1..r_G  |
       +------+  Learner                   |<----------------------+
   weight     |  - SFT on filtered samples |
   sync       |    (STaR, expert iteration)|
              |  - policy gradient with    |
              |    group baseline (GRPO)   |
              +----------------------------+

3.2 DeepSeek-R1-Zero, DeepSeek-R1, and GRPO

 V3-Base --(1) cold-start SFT on "thousands" of long-CoT examples
        --(2) reasoning RL (accuracy + language-consistency reward)
        --(3) rejection sampling: ~600k reasoning + ~200k non-reasoning
              samples (800k total); SFT V3-Base for 2 epochs
        --(4) RL for all scenarios: rule rewards for reasoning,
              preference RMs for helpfulness (judged on the final
              summary only) and harmlessness (judged on the full output)
        --> DeepSeek-R1
                 |
                 +--> distill 800k samples into Qwen2.5 / Llama 3
                      (1.5B, 7B, 8B, 14B, 32B, 70B), SFT only

5.3 SPIRAL: self-play on zero-sum language games

   SPIRAL / AZR style loop (no external dataset)

   +-----------+  proposes task / plays role A   +-------------+
   |  Policy   |-------------------------------->|  Executor / |
   |  (shared) |<--------------------------------|  game engine|
   +-----+-----+  solves task / plays role B     +------+------+
         ^                                              |
         |      per-role advantages (RAE) or            |
         +------ learnability + accuracy rewards <------+

6.3 DeepSeekMath-V2: closing the loop between generator and verifier

   +-------------+   proofs    +------------+  analyses  +---------------+
   |  Generator  |------------>|  Verifier  |----------->| Meta-verifier |
   |  (proof +   |<------------|  (score    |<-----------| (are the found|
   |  self-      |   R_Y, R_Z  |  0/0.5/1 + |  R_meta    |  issues real?)|
   |  analysis)  |             |  issues)   |            +---------------+
   +------+------+             +-----+------+
          |  new hard proofs         ^
          v                          | retrain on auto-labeled data
   +------------------------------------------------+
   | Auto-labeling: n verifications per proof;      |
   | m meta-verifications per flagged issue;        |
   | label = lowest score confirmed by >= k valid   |
   | analyses; else 1 if no issues; else to humans  |
   +------------------------------------------------+

7.3 Architectural pattern: environment as a first-class service

 +------------------+   tasks + rubrics    +------------------------------+
 |  Environment hub |--------------------->|  Rollout service             |

 |  judges)         |  actions / code /    |  partial rollouts            |
 +--------+---------+  proofs              +--------------+---------------+
          | rewards                                       | trajectories
          v                                               v
 +------------------+   batches            +------------------------------+
 |  Data buffer /   |--------------------->|  Trainer (Megatron / FSDP2)  |
 |  replay; filters |                      |  GRPO / DAPO / CISPO / ...   |
 |  (zero-variance, |                      +--------------+---------------+
 |  staleness)      |                                     | weights

          ^           (broadcast, delta sync, Mooncake)    |
          +------------------------------------------------+

05. Online and Continual Learning in Production: Streaming Recommenders, Bandit Loops, Forgetting, Continual Pretraining and Test-Time Training

Overview

  LOOP LATENCY SPECTRUM (time from outcome to parameter change)

  months      weeks       days        hours      minutes     seconds     one request
    |           |           |           |           |           |            |
  continual   scheduled   daily        hourly     streaming   bandit      test-time
  pretraining batch CT    retrain +    incremen-  online      policy      training /
  (TiC-LM,    (MLOps L1)  online LR    tal fine-  training    update      TTT layers,
  rewarm +                (Facebook)   tune       (Monolith,  (Decision   Titans, ARC
  replay)                              (Huyen 3)  FTRL)       Service)    TTT, SEAL
  <------- forgetting risk dominates -------><---- staleness/stability risk ------>

2.2 Architecture

  user actions (clicks, plays, likes)            features at serve time
              |                                         |
              v                                         v
        Kafka: action queue                     Kafka: feature queue
              \                                        /
               \                                      /
                v                                    v
         +--------------------------------------------------+
         |  JOINER (Flink)                                  |
         |  key = unique request id                         |
         |  in-memory cache + on-disk KV for features whose |
         |  actions have not arrived yet                    |
         |  negative sampling applied here                  |
         +--------------------------------------------------+
                              |
                              v
                 Kafka: joined training examples ----> also dumped to HDFS
                              |                         (batch training / replay)
                              v
         +-----------------------------------------+
         |  ONLINE TRAINING                        |
         |  workers <-> training PS                |
         |  - collisionless cuckoo-hash embeddings |
         |  - occurrence filter, expiry            |
         |  - touched-key set (ids since last sync)|
         +-----------------------------------------+
               | sparse: only touched keys,     | dense: full, on a
               | minute-level cadence           | slower cadence
               v                                v
         +-----------------------------------------+
         |  SERVING PS (embedding KV)  <- entry    |
         |  servers run the dense graph and call   |
         |  PS for embeddings                      |
         +-----------------------------------------+

3.3 Conversion delays: Alibaba ES-DFM (2021)

  impression t0 ----------------------------------------------------> time
       |          click at t0+20s              conversion at t0+3d
       |               |                              |
  Facebook (wait W):  [==== W ====] emit (pos/neg)    (missed if > W)
  Twitter (FN):       emit NEG at t0 | emit POS copy at click | loss corrects
  ES-DFM:             [= e =] emit; importance weights for later conversions
  Monolith:           Flink join by request id; on-disk KV holds unjoined features

5.2 Microsoft Decision Service and MSN (2016-2017)

            +-----------+    context x     +--------------------+
  request ->|  EXPLORE  |----------------->|  policy pi_t       |
            | (eps-grdy,|<-- action a,  ---|  (VW learner)      |
            |  bagging, |    p(a|x)        +--------------------+
            |  softmax) |                           ^
            +-----------+                           | deploy new pi_{t+1}
                  | (x, a, p)                       | (minutes)
                  v                                 |
            +-----------+  join reward r  +---------+----------+
            |   LOG     |---------------->|   LEARN (online)   |
            | (x,a,p,r) |                 |   + off-policy eval|
            +-----------+                 |   (IPS / DR) for   |
                                          |   safeguards       |
                                          +--------------------+

7.5 Design pattern

  checkpoint C_k (annealed, deployed)
        |
        +-- branch from constant-LR "trunk" (infinite schedule) or re-warm from C_k
        |
        v
  data_mix = new_shard (1 - r)  +  replay of old corpora (r)   r ~ shift strength
        |
        v
  re-warm LR -> constant -> re-decay      monitor: old-domain evals (forgetting),
        |                                 new-domain evals (plasticity), stability gap
        v
  C_{k+1}  --> alignment/RL refresh --> eval gates --> deploy

8.4 Self-editing models: SEAL (MIT, 2025) and hypernetwork adapters

  TTT DESIGN SPACE
                       what is updated           objective at test time   cost/request
  TENT                 norm stats + affine       entropy                  1 step/batch
  TTT (2019)           shared encoder            rotation prediction      k steps
  TTT layers / Titans  per-sequence fast weights self-supervised recon.   O(tokens)
  ARC TTT              per-task LoRA             leave-one-out demos      full LoRA run
  SEAL                 LoRA/full via self-edit   model-written data (RL)  inner finetune
  Text-to-LoRA         generated LoRA            none (hypernetwork)      1 forward pass
  TTRL                 policy weights (RL)       majority-vote reward     RL run on test set

06. Human-in-the-Loop and Active Learning: Labeling Platforms, Weak Supervision, Annotation Workflows, and Expert Feedback Pipelines

1. Reference architecture of a human-in-the-loop (HITL) labeling loop

            +-------------------------------------------------------------+
            |                  UNLABELED POOL / STREAM                    |
            |   (logs, fleet data, API prompts, model samples, documents) |
            +------------------------------+------------------------------+
                                           |
                     +---------------------v---------------------+
                     | SELECTOR: random | uncertainty | diversity |
                     | QBC | error-mining | policy samples (RLHF)  |
                     +---------------------+---------------------+
                                           |  batch of tasks
          +--------------------------------v-------------------------------+
          | ROUTER: crowd | vendor workforce | in-house experts | LLM judge |
          |  (skills, qualification, cost, confidentiality, overlap k)     |
          +-------+--------------------------------------------+-----------+
                  |                                            |
       +----------v-----------+                     +----------v-----------+
       | ANNOTATION UI        |  pre-labels from    | PROGRAMMATIC SOURCES |
       | (+ model pre-labels, |<--- current model --| labeling functions,  |
       |  interactive tools)  |                     | heuristics, KBs, LLMs|
       +----------+-----------+                     +----------+-----------+
                  |  raw labels                                | noisy votes
       +----------v--------------------------------------------v-----------+
       | QA + AGGREGATION: gold checks, agreement, review/reject,          |
       | Dawid-Skene / label model, confident-learning error flags         |
       +----------------------------------+--------------------------------+
                                          |  labels + provenance + confidence
                        +-----------------v------------------+
                        | LABEL STORE (versioned, auditable) |
                        +-----------------+------------------+
                                          |
                        +-----------------v------------------+
                        | TRAIN / EVAL -> new model version  |----> back to SELECTOR
                        +------------------------------------+       and pre-labeler

3.3 Snorkel (VLDB 2018) architecture

  subject-matter expert
          |
          v  writes / edits
  +-----------------------+    apply over     +-----------------------------+
  | Labeling functions    |------------------>| Label matrix L (n x m)      |
  | (patterns, KB lookups,|   unlabeled data  | entries in {-1 abstain, 0,  |
  |  heuristics, models)  |                   |  ..., k-1}                  |
  +-----------------------+                   +--------------+--------------+
          ^                                                  |
          |  LF analysis: coverage, overlap, conflict,       v
          |  empirical accuracy on a small dev set   +----------------------+
          +------------------------------------------| Label model          |
                                                     | (learns LF accuracies|
                                                     |  w/o ground truth)   |
                                                     +----------+-----------+
                                                                | P(y given L)
                                                     +----------v-----------+
                                                     | End model (deep net),|
                                                     | noise-aware loss     |
                                                     +----------------------+

5.1 The workforce funnel

 applicants --> screening test --> onboarding / training tasks --> production queue
                (agreement with     (guidelines, worked          | overlap k, hidden gold
                 researchers,        examples, shared chat)      | (QC items) per batch
                 sensitivity tests)                              v
                                                     reviewer tier (fix/accept/reject)
                                                                 |
                       per-worker accuracy, agreement, speed <---+--> aggregate label
                       -> retrain, re-instruct, or remove           with confidence

7.1 Process supervision with active learning: PRM800K

 Phase 1: labelers rate steps of sampled solutions; may label alternative
          completions; may write their own correct step if all candidates fail
                                     |
 Phase 2: generation g = 1..G
   best PRM so far --scores--> large pool of pre-generated solutions
          |                      select solutions to label (active learning;
          |                      the paper targets convincing wrong-answer solutions)
          v
   labelers rate steps; STOP at first error ("found_error") -> cost saving
   + QC solutions served to every labeler, checked against gold
          |
          v
   retrain PRM on all labels -> next generation

        +-------------------------------------------------------------------> RM data

08. Self-Improving Agents and Automated Optimization: Reflection, Skill Libraries, Evolutionary Program Search, Self-Modifying Agents and Prompt Optimizers

Overview

          +--------------------------------------------------------------+
          |                  ARCHIVE / MEMORY                            |
          |  reflections | skills | programs | agent variants | prompts  |
          +-----------+-----------------------------------^--------------+
                      | select (greedy / Pareto /         | insert if valid
                      | MAP-Elites / novelty / bandit)    | (score, feedback)
                      v                                   |
          +-----------------------+   artifact   +--------+-------------+
          |  PROPOSER (LLM(s))    |------------->|  EVALUATOR           |
          |  prompt = parents +   |              |  tests / simulator / |
          |  feedback + context   |<-------------|  benchmark / judge   |
          +-----------------------+  textual     +----------------------+
                                     feedback

1.1 Reflexion (Shinn et al., 2023)

 trial t:  task --> [Actor (+ reflections memory)] --> trajectory --> [Evaluator]
                                ^                                         |
                                |                    reward / test result |
                     append     |                                         v
            [Long-term memory] <-------- [Self-Reflection LLM] <-- (trajectory, reward)
 trial t+1: same task, Actor now conditioned on the new reflection

2.1 Voyager: an executable skill library (Wang et al., 2023)

 +-------------------+  next task   +----------------------------------------+
 | Automatic         |------------->| Code generation (GPT-4)                |
 | curriculum (LLM)  |              |   prompt = task + retrieved skills +   |
 +---------^---------+              |            env feedback + errors       |
           | state, history         +-------------------+--------------------+
           |                                            | program
 +---------+---------+   success     +------------------v-------------------+
 | Skill library     |<--------------| Execute in Minecraft -> Self-verify  |
 | (code, embedded   |  commit skill | (critic LLM); on failure, re-prompt  |
 |  description)     |-------------->|  with errors (bounded rounds)        |
 +-------------------+  retrieve     +--------------------------------------+

2.6 ACE: Agentic Context Engineering (Zhang et al., 2025)

  query --> [Generator] -- trajectory, outcome --> [Reflector] -- lessons -->
                ^                                                         |
                |                                                         v
           [Playbook: itemized bullets      <-- deterministic merge -- [Curator]
            with helpful/harmful counters]       dedupe, prune          (delta ops)

3.1 FunSearch (Romera-Paredes et al., Nature 2023)

  islands:  [I1] [I2] ... [Ik]   (periodic reset of the worst islands)
     | sample k parents (score-sorted) from one island
     v
  prompt "v0 ... v(k-1); write v(k)"  -->  code LLM (many parallel samplers)
                                               |
                                               v
                                   sandboxed evaluators (score or reject)
                                               |
                       valid program + score --+--> back into the same island

3.2 AlphaEvolve (Novikov et al., Google DeepMind, 2025)

 Human: evaluate(h) + initial program (EVOLVE-BLOCKs) + context
                         |
                         v
 +-----------------+  prompt  +------------------------+  diffs  +----------------+
 | Prompt sampler  |--------->| LLM ensemble           |-------->| Apply diff ->  |
 | parents,        |          | Flash: many samples    |         | new program    |
 | inspirations,   |          | Pro: few, high quality |         +-------+--------+
 | meta-prompts    |          +------------------------+                 |
 +--------^--------+                                                     v
          |                                     +--------------------------------+
          |      programs + multi-metric scores | Evaluator cascade (cheap ->    |
 +--------+-----------------------+<------------| expensive), LLM feedback,      |
 | Evolutionary DB (MAP-Elites x  |             | async parallel eval cluster    |
 | islands)                       |             +--------------------------------+
 +--------------------------------+

4.5 Darwin Gödel Machine (Zhang, Hu, Lu, Lange, Clune; Sakana AI and UBC, 2025)

 Archive A = {agent_0}
 repeat G=80 generations:
   parents <- sample(A, weight = sigmoid(score) / (1 + n_children))
   for p in parents (parallel):
       task  <- pick failure from p's eval logs (unresolved / empty patch / ...)
       child <- p modifies ITS OWN repo (diagnose -> propose feature -> implement)
       if compiles and passes small subset:
            evaluate on medium (score > 0.4) -> full (score > archive threshold)
            A <- A + {child}            # keep_all: stepping stones retained

5.6 GEPA: Reflective Prompt Evolution (Agrawal et al., 2025)

 Pareto pool P (per-instance winners)
     | sample parent
     v
 run on minibatch --> full traces + ASI --> reflection LLM: diagnose -> rewrite module
     ^                                                        |
     |                     improved on minibatch?             v
     +--------- yes: add child, update per-instance front <---+
                no : discard

5.8 A production pattern: the self-evolving agent loop (OpenAI and Bain cookbook, 2025)

 prod/eval inputs --> agent(prompt v_k) --> output --> graders (rule + embed + judge)
                                                          |
                    pass (lenient aggregate) <------------+------> fail
                         |                                          |
                  ship v_k (versioned)          metaprompt agent rewrites -> v_{k+1}
                                                (<= N retries, else page a human)

09. Closed-Loop Robotics and Control: Sim-to-Real Loops, Robot Foundation Model Data Flywheels, Teleoperation, DAgger, and World Models

1. Reference architecture: nested loops around a robot policy

 +-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | L4  WORLD-MODEL / SIM LOOP (hours-days)                                           |
 |   physics sim (Isaac Lab, MuJoCo)   learned video/latent world model (Cosmos,     |
 |   domain randomization, real2sim      V-JEPA 2-AC, Genie-style)                   |
 |       |  synthetic demos / RL rollouts / policy evaluation scores        ^        |
 +-------|------------------------------------------------------------------|--------+
         v                                                                  |
 +-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | L3  FLEET DATA FLYWHEEL (days-months)                                             |
 |   teleop rigs ---> episode store ---> curation/filtering ---> pretrain/post-train |
 |   (ALOHA, DROID,   (RLDS, LeRobot)    (idle filter, success   generalist VLA      |
 |    UMI, VR)              ^             labels, relabeling)          |             |
 |                          |                                          v             |
 |                autonomous rollouts <---- deploy checkpoint to fleet <---          |
 +--------------------------^--------------------------------------------------------+
                            |
 +--------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------+
 | L2  CORRECTION LOOP (seconds-minutes)                                             |
 |   human / supervisor watches rollout -> takes over (gated) -> correction labels   |
 |   reward classifier / value function -> RL update (HIL-SERL, Recap)               |
 +--------------------------^--------------------------------------------------------+
                            |
 +--------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------+
 | L1  POLICY LOOP (3-50 Hz VLA; up to 200 Hz fast head)                             |
 |   images + proprio + language --> VLA --> action chunk (H steps)                  |
 | L0  SERVO LOOP (hundreds of Hz - kHz): joint PD / whole-body controller tracks it |
 +-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

5. Physical Intelligence: the π model family as a closed loop

   cross-embodiment robot data (10k+ h)     web / VLM data     verbal & subtask labels
                 \                               |                    /
                  v                              v                   v
          +-----------------------------------------------------------------+
          | PRETRAIN  VLM backbone (PaliGemma) + action expert              |
          |           pi0: flow matching;  pi0-FAST: FAST tokens;           |
          |           pi0.5: co-training + knowledge insulation             |
          +-------------------------------+---------------------------------+
                                          | post-train (task demos)
                                          v
          +-----------------------------------------------------------------+
          | DEPLOY  -> autonomous episodes + expert teleop corrections      |
          +-------------------------------+---------------------------------+
                                          | pi*0.6 "Recap"
                                          v
          +-----------------------------------------------------------------+
          | value function fit on all data -> advantage per step            |
          | policy re-trained conditioned on advantage indicator            |
          | (demos + autonomous rollouts + interventions) -> redeploy       |
          +-----------------------------------------------------------------+

6. Autonomous fleet data collection: AutoRT

 explore map -> pick point of interest -> drive there
        |
        v
 VLM describes scene + objects ---> LLM proposes tasks
        |
        v
 LLM critic filters tasks with a "robot constitution"
 (foundational / safety / embodiment rules) and routes each to:
     [autonomous policy]  or  [human teleop]  or  [reject]
        |
        v
 execute -> score episode diversity vs prior data -> repeat

7.2 SERL and HIL-SERL: on-robot RL with humans in the loop

 1. teleop positive/negative examples -> train binary REWARD CLASSIFIER
 2. collect small demo set ------------> seed DEMO BUFFER
 3. online:  ACTOR (robot) --transitions--> LEARNER (off-policy RL, SAC-style)
                ^   human intervention        |
                |   (frequent early,          | periodic weight sync
                |    tapering later)          v
                +------------------------- updated policy

 EXPERIMENT MANAGER
   stage 1: preliminary implementation   \   each stage = best-first tree search:
   stage 2: hyperparameter tuning         >   num_drafts roots; num_workers parallel
   stage 3: research agenda execution    /    expansions; buggy nodes debugged with
   stage 4: ablations                         prob debug_prob up to max_debug_depth;
        |                                     best node seeds the next stage
        v
 plot aggregation (VLM checks figures) --> write-up (o1-class model) --> AI review

11. Risks of Closed Loops: Model Collapse, Reward Hacking, Filter Bubbles, Goodhart's Law and Sycophancy

Overview

     +-----------------------------------------------------------------+
     |                                                                 |
     v                                                                 |
 +--------+   outputs    +-------------+   measured   +-----------+    |
 | model  +------------->+ world/users +------------->+  proxy    |    |
 | (theta)|              | / grader /  |   signal     |  signal   |    |
 +---^----+              | corpus      |              | (reward,  |    |
     |                   +-------------+              |  clicks,  |    |
     |                         ^                      |  data)    |    |
     |                         | model output changes +-----+-----+    |
     |                         | what is measured           |          |
     |   update toward proxy   +----------------------------+          |
     +-----------------------------------------------------------------+

 Failure appears when: (a) the proxy drifts from intent under optimization
 pressure (Goodhart, reward hacking, sycophancy), or (b) the model's own
 outputs reshape the data it later learns from (collapse, filter bubbles).

2.5 Architecture of a collapse-resistant data loop

  real data (human, sensors, logs) ----------------------------------+
        |   tag: provenance=human, time, source                      |
        v                                                            |
  +-------------+      sample with      +----------------+           |
  | model gen t +---- controlled temp ->+ synthetic pool |           |
  +------^------+                       | tag: model, t, |           |
         |                              | filter score   |           |
         |                              +-------+--------+           |
         |                                      | verify / filter    |
         |                                      v                    v
         |                         +-------------------------------------+
         |                         | training mix for gen t+1             |
         |                         |  - ALL real data (never discarded)   |
         |                         |  - accumulated synthetic, capped     |
         |                         |  - fixed min. fraction fresh real    |
         |                         +------------------+------------------+
         |                                            |
         |   gate: tail-coverage, diversity, rare-    |
         +---- class recall vs frozen real eval set <-+

5.1 The loop

 user state u_t ---> recommender picks slate S_t ---> user clicks c_t in S_t
      ^                       ^                               |
      |  exposure shifts      | trained on (S, c) pairs       |
      |  interests            | only from shown items         v
      +----------------- u_{t+1} = f(u_t, S_t) <------ logged data D_t

6.2 Case study: the GPT-4o rollback (April 2025)

  ChatGPT conversations
        |
        +--> thumbs up/down ----------+   (new, product-scale signal)
        |                             v
        |                   +-------------------+
        |                   | reward mixture    |   primary RM held
        |                   |  primary RM  (w1) |<- sycophancy in check
        |                   |  user-fb RM  (w2) |
        |                   |  other sig.  (w3) |
        |                   +---------+---------+
        |                             v
        |                      RL post-training
        |                             v
        |         gates: offline evals OK | A/B tests OK | experts: "felt off"
        |                             v
        +<---------------- launch (quantitative signals won) ------------+

7.1 A layered mitigation architecture

 +----------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | L0 SPEC      model spec / constitution; behavior-specific rules [37][38]   |
 +----------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | L1 DATA      provenance tags; accumulate (never replace) real data;        |
 |              fresh real fraction; exploration + propensity logging         |
 |              [1][4][40][48]                                                |
 +----------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | L2 REWARD    reward mixture with reviewed weights; RM ensembles / WARM;    |
 |              rule-based rewards; length control; verifier hardening;       |
 |              meta-verification [13][14][16][50]                            |
 +----------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | L3 TRAINING  KL budget + early stop on gold/held-out signal; inoculation   |
 |              prompting; no optimization pressure on CoT [12][20][22]       |
 +----------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | L4 MONITOR   CoT + action monitors; hack-rate dashboards; proxy-vs-gold    |
 |              divergence alarms; diversity / tail metrics [3][21][22]       |
 +----------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | L5 EVAL/GATE behavior evals (sycophancy, delusion, reward hacking);        |
 |              automated audits; stratified by user type; launch-blocking    |
 |              qualitative review; alpha cohort [31][33][34][36]             |
 +----------------------------------------------------------------------------+
 | L6 DEPLOY    staged rollout; fast rollback (~24h for GPT-4o); product      |
 |              safeguards (classifiers, crisis banners) [31][33]             |
 +----------------------------------------------------------------------------+

2.1 TFX (Google): the validated-pipeline reference design

ExampleGen -> StatisticsGen -> SchemaGen -> ExampleValidator
     |                                          |
     +--------------> Transform -----------> Trainer (+ Tuner)
                                                 |
           Resolver(LatestBlessedModel) -> Evaluator --(blessing)--+
                                                 |                  |
                                         InfraValidator --(infra)--+--> Pusher
                         every step records artifacts/executions in MLMD

3.2 Chronon: measuring online/offline consistency with logs

online fetch --> log {key bytes, value bytes, ts, caller}  (sampled by KEY HASH)
                       | partitioned by date + join
                       v
Spark job: filter logs -> replace Join's left source with logged keys/ts
                       -> recompute offline join for same period
                       v
compare logged (a) vs backfilled (b) per feature:
   equality: mismatch %, missing %, extra %
   numeric : symmetric % diff, delta distribution
   sequence: length dist, Levenshtein (ins/del separately)
                       v
Hive table per join (date, time, group-by) with ReqSketch quantiles

6.2 Designing the trigger controller

 signals: [new span]  [drift score]  [est. perf]  [realized perf]  [cron]
              \            |              |              |            /
               v           v              v              v           v
         +-------------------------------------------------------------+
         | TRIGGER POLICY                                              |
         |  - debounce / min interval between runs                     |
         |  - require data-validation PASS before training             |
         |  - weight drift by estimated impact (NannyML-style)         |
         |  - dedupe events (multi-event window, Metaflow [22])        |
         |  - acyclic trigger graph; cap concurrent runs               |
         +----------------------------+--------------------------------+
                                      v
                         CT pipeline run (data version pinned)
                                      v
                 gates: data -> model vs baseline -> infra -> registry

7.3 Metric-gated progressive delivery

 weight:  0% --10%--> [analysis] --25%--> [analysis] --50%--> ... --100%
                        |   success: continue   failure x failureLimit
                        |                         |
                        +--------> ABORT: weight=0, stable still scaled
                                   registry alias stays on previous version

            Sectioning                         Voting

            +-----------------------------------------------------+
            |                                                     |
